18
]
and
[
26
]
, but is performed at the paradifferential level.
Precisely, we define the new variables

(\tilde{w}_{1}^{s},\tilde{r}_{1}^{s}):=(T_{\Phi}|D|^{s}w,T_{\Phi}|D|^{s}r),

where the conjugation function
\Phi
satisfies the differential equation

(4.11)

D_{t}\Phi=s\Phi{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}+O_{\underline{A}}(\underline{A}^{2}_{\frac{1}{4}}).

We choose the real-valued conjugation function
\Phi
by

\Phi:=|1+{\mathbf{W}}|^{-2s}e^{-s\frac{\gamma}{g}\Re R-s\frac{\gamma^{2}}{g}\Im W}.

Indeed, one can compute

\displaystyle D_{t}\Phi

\displaystyle=\partial_{{\mathbf{W}}}\Phi D_{t}{\mathbf{W}}+\partial_{\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}}\Phi D_{t}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}

\displaystyle=s\Phi\left(R_{\alpha}(1-\bar{Y})+\bar{R}_{\alpha}(1-Y)-2\underline{M}+\gamma\Im{\mathbf{W}}(Y+\bar{Y})-\frac{\gamma}{g}D_{t}\Re R-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{g}D_{t}\Im W\right)

\displaystyle=s\Phi\left({\underline{b}}_{\alpha}-\underline{M}+\gamma\Im{\mathbf{W}}(Y+\bar{Y}-1)+\gamma\Im{\mathbf{W}}\right)+O_{\underline{A}}(\underline{A}^{2}_{\frac{1}{4}})

\displaystyle=s\Phi{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}+O_{\underline{A}}(\underline{A}^{2}_{\frac{1}{4}}),

where we use the fact that

\gamma D_{t}\Re R=-g\gamma\Im{\mathbf{W}}+\gamma^{2}\Im R+O_{\underline{A}}(\underline{A}^{2}_{\frac{1}{4}}),\quad\gamma^{2}D_{t}\Im W=-\gamma^{2}\Im R+O_{\underline{A}}(\underline{A}^{2}_{\frac{1}{4}}),

and also (
1.8
), (
3.7
).
We then apply the para-product rule (
2.12
) for
\Phi{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}
to get

T_{D_{t}}\tilde{w}_{1}^{s}=T_{\Phi}T_{D_{t}}w^{s}+sT_{{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}}\tilde{w}_{1}^{s}+G_{s}.

Similarly, we have

T_{D_{t}}\tilde{r}_{1}^{s}=T_{\Phi}T_{D_{t}}r^{s}+sT_{{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}}\tilde{r}_{1}^{s}+K_{s}.

For the other terms on the left-hand side of (
4.10
), we use (
2.11
) and (
2.12
) to write

\displaystyle T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{r}^{s}_{1}=T_{\Phi}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r^{s}-s(T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{(1+{\mathbf{W}})Y_{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma}{g}\Re R_{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{g}\Im{\mathbf{W}}}+T_{\bar{Y}_{\alpha}})\tilde{r}^{s}_{1}+G_{s},

\displaystyle T_{(1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}+\gamma\Im{\mathbf{W}}}\tilde{w}^{s}_{1}=T_{\Phi}T_{(1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}+\gamma\Im{\mathbf{W}}}w^{s}_{1}+G_{s}

in the first equation, and

T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\tilde{w}^{s}_{1}=T_{\Phi}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}w^{s}_{1}+K_{s}

in the second equation.

T_{\Phi}
commutes with the coefficients on the right side of (
4.10
) modulo acceptable errors.
Therefore, for
(\tilde{w}^{s}_{1},\tilde{r}^{s}_{1})
we write

(4.12)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}T_{D_{t}}\tilde{w}^{s}_{1}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{r}^{s}_{1}+T_{(1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}+\gamma\Im{\mathbf{W}}}\tilde{w}^{s}_{1}=\mathcal{G}^{s}_{1}+G_{s}&\\
T_{D_{t}}\tilde{r}^{s}_{1}+i\gamma\tilde{r}^{s}_{1}-iT_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\tilde{w}^{s}_{1}=\mathcal{K}^{s}_{1}+K_{s},&\end{array}\right.

with the nonperturbative source terms

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{s}_{1}=

\displaystyle L({\underline{b}}_{\alpha},\tilde{w}^{s}_{1})+sT_{{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}}\tilde{w}^{s}_{1}-L(\bar{Y}_{\alpha},\tilde{r}^{s}_{1})-sT_{\bar{Y}_{\alpha}}\tilde{r}^{s}_{1}

\displaystyle+L([(1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}]_{\alpha}+\gamma\Im{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\tilde{w}^{s}_{1})-sT_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{(1+{\mathbf{W}})Y_{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma}{g}\Re R_{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{g}\Im{\mathbf{W}}}\tilde{r}^{s}_{1},

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{s}_{1}=

\displaystyle L({\underline{b}}_{\alpha},\tilde{r}^{s}_{1})+sT_{{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}}\tilde{r}^{s}_{1}+iL(Y_{\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\tilde{w}^{s}_{1}).

The first two leading terms in both
\mathcal{G}^{s}_{1}
and
\mathcal{K}^{s}_{1}
cancel.
More precisely, define the bilinear term

L_{1}(f_{\alpha\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}u)=L(f_{\alpha},u)+sT_{f_{\alpha}}u\approx-s(s-1)T_{f_{\alpha\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}u+\text{lower order terms},

we can rewrite

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{s}_{1}=

\displaystyle L_{1}({\underline{b}}_{\alpha\alpha},\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}\tilde{w}^{s}_{1})-L_{1}(\bar{Y}_{\alpha\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\tilde{r}^{s}_{1})+L([(1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}]_{\alpha}+\gamma\Im{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\tilde{w}^{s}_{1})

\displaystyle-sT_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{(1+{\mathbf{W}})Y_{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma}{g}\Re R_{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{g}\Im{\mathbf{W}}}\tilde{r}^{s}_{1},

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{s}_{1}=

\displaystyle L_{1}({\underline{b}}_{\alpha\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\tilde{r}^{s}_{1})+iL(Y_{\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\tilde{w}^{s}_{1}).

Here the source terms
(\mathcal{G}^{s}_{1},\mathcal{K}^{s}_{1})
satisfy the estimate

\||D|^{-\frac{1}{4}}(\mathcal{G}^{s}_{1},\mathcal{K}^{s}_{1})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\lesssim_{\underline{A}}{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{s}}.

We claim that

\displaystyle\|(\tilde{w}_{1}^{s},\tilde{r}_{1}^{s})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\approx_{{\underline{A}}}\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{s}},

\displaystyle\|(\tilde{w}_{1}^{s},\tilde{r}_{1}^{s})-|D|^{s}(w,r)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\lesssim_{\underline{A}}{\underline{A}}\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{s}}.

In fact, it is shown in
[
13
]
and
[
4
]
that

\|(T_{|1+{\mathbf{W}}|^{-2s}}\tilde{w}^{s},T_{|1+{\mathbf{W}}|^{-2s}}\tilde{r}^{s})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\approx_{{\underline{A}}}\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{s}},

and also the corresponding difference bound.
For the other exponential factor,

\Big|-s\frac{\gamma}{g}\Re R-s\frac{\gamma^{2}}{g}\Im W\Big|\leq\frac{s}{g}(\gamma|\Re R|+\gamma^{2}|\Im W|)\lesssim_{s,g}{\underline{A}}.

As long as
{\underline{A}}\ll 1
, the exponential factor is harmless, and does not contribute too much to the
\mathcal{H}^{0}
norm.

Next, we proceed with additional normal form corrections.
The normal form corrections not only replace bilinear source terms by trilinear terms, but also turn trilinear and higher unbalanced interactions into balanced ones.
Our normal form corrections will consist of the
L_{1}
bilinear corrections which eliminate the
L_{1}
terms, and also the secondary corrections which eliminate the other terms.

We begin with the corrections consisting of
L_{1}
bilinear forms.
Heuristically, at the paradifferential level, we use (
1.8
) and the para-material derivatives (
3.9
) to write

\displaystyle{\underline{b}}_{\alpha\alpha}

\displaystyle\approx 2\Re T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha\alpha}+\gamma\Im T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}\approx-2\Re T_{1-Y}T_{D_{t}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}-\gamma\Im T_{1-\bar{Y}}(iT_{D_{t}}R_{\alpha}-\gamma R_{\alpha})

\displaystyle=-2\Re T_{1-Y}T_{D_{t}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}-\frac{\gamma}{g}\Re T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{D_{t}}R_{\alpha}-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{g}\Im T_{1-Y}T_{D_{t}}{\mathbf{W}}.

This motivates us to set

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\tilde{w}^{s}_{2}=L_{1}(2\Re T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma}{g}\Re T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{g}\Im T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}},\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\tilde{w}^{s}_{1})&\\
\tilde{r}^{s}_{2}=L_{1}(2\Re T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma}{g}\Re T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{g}\Im T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}},\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\tilde{r}^{s}_{1}).&\end{array}\right.

We claim that this correction has the following effect

(4.13)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}T_{D_{t}}(\tilde{w}^{s}_{1}+\tilde{w}^{s}_{2})+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}(\tilde{r}^{s}_{1}+\tilde{r}^{s}_{2})+T_{(1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}+\gamma{\mathbf{W}}}(\tilde{w}^{s}_{1}+\tilde{w}^{s}_{2})=\mathcal{G}^{s}_{2}+G_{s}&\\
T_{D_{t}}(\tilde{r}^{s}_{1}+\tilde{r}^{s}_{2})+i\gamma(\tilde{r}^{s}_{1}+\tilde{r}^{s}_{2})-iT_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}(\tilde{w}^{s}_{1}+\tilde{w}^{s}_{2})=\mathcal{K}^{s}_{2}+K_{s},&\end{array}\right.

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{s}_{2}=L\left([(1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}]_{\alpha}+\gamma\Im{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\tilde{w}^{s}_{1}\right)+L\left((1+{\mathbf{W}})Y_{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma}{g}\Re R_{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{g}\Im{\mathbf{W}},T_{1-\bar{Y}}\tilde{r}^{s}_{1}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{s}_{2}=iL(Y_{\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\tilde{w}^{s}_{1}).

To prove this we begin with the first equation of the system.
When applying the para-material derivatives
T_{D_{t}}
, we use either (
2.17
) or (
2.18
) to distribute it to the paraproducts.
The commutators with
\partial_{\alpha}
involve
T_{{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}}
, and we use (
3.4
) to place it into the error term
G_{s}
.
We compute

\displaystyle T_{D_{t}}\tilde{w}^{s}_{2}=

\displaystyle-L_{1}\left(2\Re T_{T_{D_{t}}Y}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma}{g}\Re T_{T_{D_{t}}\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{g}\Im T_{T_{D_{t}}Y}{\mathbf{W}},\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\tilde{w}^{s}_{1}\right)

\displaystyle+L_{1}\left(2\Re T_{1-Y}T_{D_{t}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma}{g}\Re T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{D_{t}}R_{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{g}\Im T_{1-Y}T_{D_{t}}{\mathbf{W}},\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\tilde{w}^{s}_{1}\right)

\displaystyle+L_{1}\left(2\Re T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma}{g}\Re T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{g}\Im T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}},\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}T_{D_{t}}\tilde{w}^{s}_{1}\right)+G_{s}.

The first term can be added into
G_{s}
in view of (
3.10
).
The second term becomes
L_{1}({\underline{b}}_{\alpha\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\tilde{w}^{s}_{1})+G_{s}
.
For the third term, we use (
4.12
) together with the source term bounds to handle
T_{D_{t}}\tilde{w}_{1}^{s}
.
These bounds allow us to estimate the corresponding
L_{1}
contributions by taking advantage of the fact that
L_{1}
has a paraproduct structure.
As a consequence,

T_{D_{t}}\tilde{w}^{s}_{2}=-L_{1}({\underline{b}}_{\alpha\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\tilde{w}^{s}_{1})-L_{1}\left(2\Re T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma}{g}\Re T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{g}\Im T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}},T_{1-\bar{Y}}\tilde{r}^{s}_{1}\right)+G_{s}.

For the remaining two terms on the left-hand side of the first equation,
we repeatedly apply (
2.11
) and (
2.12
) so that the error terms can be absorbed into
G_{s}
.
Also, when derivatives fall on the coefficients, we have a good balance of derivatives.
We get

\displaystyle T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{r}^{s}_{2}=

\displaystyle L_{1}\left(2\Re T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma}{g}\Re T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{g}\Im T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}},T_{1-\bar{Y}}\tilde{r}^{s}_{1}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle L_{1}(\bar{Y}_{\alpha\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\tilde{w}^{s}_{1})+L_{1}\left({\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}+\frac{\gamma}{g}R_{\alpha\alpha}-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{g}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha},T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\tilde{r}^{s}_{1}\right)+G_{s},

\displaystyle T_{(1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}+\gamma\Im{\mathbf{W}}}\tilde{w}^{s}_{2}=

\displaystyle G_{s}.

We repeat above computation for the second equation of the system.
After simplification,

\displaystyle T_{D_{t}}\tilde{r}^{s}_{2}=L_{1}\left(2\Re T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma}{g}\Re T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{g}\Im T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}},\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}T_{D_{t}}\tilde{r}^{s}_{1}\right)-L_{1}({\underline{b}}_{\alpha\alpha},\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\tilde{r}^{s}_{1})+K_{s},

\displaystyle-iT_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\tilde{w}^{s}_{2}=L_{1}\left(2\Re T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma}{g}\Re T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{g}\Im T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}},-iT_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\tilde{w}^{s}_{1}\right)+K_{s},

\displaystyle i\gamma\tilde{r}^{s}_{2}=L_{1}\left(2\Re T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma}{g}\Re T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{g}\Im T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}},i\gamma\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\tilde{r}^{s}_{1}\right)+K_{s}.

Collecting and cancelling all these contributions, and extracting perturbative paradifferential terms into
(G_{s},K_{s})
, all
L_{1}
terms in
(\mathcal{G}^{s}_{1},\mathcal{K}^{s}_{1})
cancel, and we prove the claim for (
4.13
).

Then we consider the corrections consisting of the remaining
L
bilinear forms in the secondary source term
(\mathcal{G}^{s}_{2},\mathcal{K}^{s}_{2})
.
For
(\tilde{w}^{s}_{3},\tilde{r}^{s}_{3})
defined by

\displaystyle\tilde{w}^{s}_{3}:=L\left((1+{\mathbf{W}})Y_{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma}{g}\Re R_{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{g}\Im{\mathbf{W}},\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\tilde{w}^{s}_{1}\right),

\displaystyle\tilde{r}^{s}_{3}:=0,

they are easily checked to cancel the secondary source terms
(\mathcal{G}^{s}_{2},\mathcal{K}^{s}_{2})
.

Finally, by using the paraproduct estimate (
2.2
) to redistribute the derivatives if necessary, the normal form corrections satisfy the bound

\|(\tilde{w}^{s}_{2}+\tilde{w}^{s}_{3},\tilde{r}^{s}_{2}+\tilde{r}^{s}_{3})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\lesssim_{\underline{A}}{\underline{A}}\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{s}}.

Let

\displaystyle\tilde{w}^{s}=\tilde{w}^{s}_{1}+\tilde{w}^{s}_{2}+\tilde{w}^{s}_{3},

\displaystyle\tilde{r}^{s}=\tilde{r}^{s}_{1}+\tilde{r}^{s}_{2}+\tilde{r}^{s}_{3},

then
(\tilde{w}^{s},\tilde{r}^{s})
solve (
4.5
) with the desired bounds, and this finishes the proof of the proposition.
∎

4.3.
The bounds for the paradifferential source terms

In this subsection, we first compute the paradifferential representation of the source terms
(\underline{\mathcal{G}}_{0},\underline{\mathcal{K}}_{0})
and
(\underline{\mathcal{G}}_{1},\underline{\mathcal{K}}_{1})
.
Then we compute the leading terms of para-material derivatives of
w,r
and auxiliary functions
x=T_{1-Y}w
,
u=T_{1-Y}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}w
.

Lemma 4.7
.

The source terms
(\underline{\mathcal{G}}_{0},\underline{\mathcal{K}}_{0})
satisfy the bound

(4.14)

\|(\underline{\mathcal{G}}_{0},\underline{\mathcal{K}}_{0})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\lesssim_{A^{\sharp}}{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left(\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{4}}}+\gamma\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{-\frac{1}{4}}}\right).

Moreover, they have the representation

{\mathbf{P}}\underline{\mathcal{G}}_{0}=\mathcal{G}_{0,0}+G,\quad{\mathbf{P}}\underline{\mathcal{K}}_{0}=\mathcal{K}_{0,0}+K,

where
(G,K)
are perturbative in the sense that they satisfy the quadratic bound

\|(G,K)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\lesssim_{{\underline{A}^{\sharp}}}{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}{\underline{A}^{\sharp}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left(\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{4}}}+\gamma\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{-\frac{1}{4}}}\right).

The main parts of the source terms
(\mathcal{G}_{0,0},\mathcal{K}_{0,0})
are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{0,0}=-{\mathbf{P}}[T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{r}_{\alpha}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}Y]+{\mathbf{P}}[T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{x}_{\alpha}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R]-i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbf{P}}[\bar{w}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}Y+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{x}_{\alpha}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}W],

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{0,0}=-{\mathbf{P}}[T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{r}_{\alpha}R]-i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbf{P}}[\bar{w}R-T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{r}_{\alpha}W].

Proof.

Recall that

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{G}}_{0}(w,r)=\mathcal{G}(w,r)+i\frac{\gamma}{2}(1+\mathbf{W})(\mathbf{P}\bar{m}_{1}-\bar{\mathbf{P}}m_{1}),

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{K}}_{0}(w,r)=\mathcal{K}(w,r)-i\frac{\gamma}{2}(\bar{\mathbf{P}}m_{2}+\mathbf{P}\bar{m}_{2}),

where

m_{1}=(1-\bar{Y})w-(1-Y)^{2}\bar{W}w_{\alpha},\quad m_{2}=\bar{R}w-(1-Y)\bar{W}(r_{\alpha}+R_{\alpha}w).

The corresponding analysis for
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{K})
is already carried out in Lemma
5.3

of
[
4
]
, the only difference compared to
[
4
]
is that we can put
-{\mathbf{P}}(T_{\bar{x}}\bar{R}_{\alpha}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)
into
G
, and
-{\mathbf{P}}(T_{\bar{x}}\bar{R}_{\alpha}R)
into
K
since we use the control norm
{\underline{A}^{\sharp}_{\frac{1}{4}}}
.
It suffices to compute the vorticity terms.

We consider
-i\frac{\gamma}{2}\mathcal{G}_{1}(w,r)
term first.
We claim that the term

-i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbf{P}}((1+{\mathbf{W}})\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}m_{1})=-i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbf{P}}({\mathbf{W}}\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}m_{1})

is perturbative.
Since by using the commutator structure and Lemma
2.1
,

\|{\mathbf{P}}({\mathbf{W}}\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}m_{1})\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}}=\||D|^{\frac{1}{4}}[{\mathbf{P}},{\mathbf{W}}]\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}m_{1}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\||D|^{\frac{1}{4}}{\mathbf{W}}\|_{BMO}\|\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}m_{1}\|_{L^{2}},

it only remains to show that

(4.15)

\gamma\|\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}m_{1}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{\underline{A}}{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left(\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}}+\gamma\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{-\frac{1}{4}}}\right).

After expanding the
m_{1}
expression, we apply the Lemma
2.1
,

\displaystyle\gamma\|\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}(\bar{Y}w)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\gamma\|[\bar{{\mathbf{P}}},\bar{Y}]w\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\gamma\||D|^{\frac{1}{4}}Y\|_{BMO}\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{-\frac{1}{4}}}\lesssim_{\underline{A}}\gamma{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{-\frac{1}{4}}},

\displaystyle\gamma\|\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}(\bar{W}w_{\alpha})\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|[\bar{{\mathbf{P}}},\bar{W}]w_{\alpha}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\gamma\||D|^{\frac{3}{4}}W\|_{BMO}\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\lesssim_{\underline{A}}{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}},

\displaystyle\gamma\|\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}(Y\bar{W}w_{\alpha})\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|Y\|_{L^{\infty}}\gamma\|\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}(\bar{W}w_{\alpha})\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{\underline{A}}{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}}.

Next, for the
i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbf{P}}((1+{\mathbf{W}}){\mathbf{P}}\bar{m}_{1})
term, the high-low and high-high components are balanced owing to (
2.1
) and (
4.15
),
so that

i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbf{P}}((1+{\mathbf{W}}){\mathbf{P}}\bar{m}_{1})=i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}{\mathbf{P}}\bar{m}_{1}+G,

where
G
is perturbative.
We then write

{\mathbf{P}}\bar{m}_{1}=-{\mathbf{P}}[Y\bar{w}+(1-\bar{Y})^{2}W\bar{w}_{\alpha}].

For each cubic term, when the lowest frequency variable is differentiated, it may be absorbed into
G
.
In particular,
Y
and
W
cannot have the lowest frequency due to the Littlewood-Paley projection
{\mathbf{P}}
.
Also, when
\bar{w}_{\alpha}
has the lowest frequency, the term is perturbative.
Therefore, after applying (
2.16
) we obtain

i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbf{P}}((1+{\mathbf{W}}){\mathbf{P}}\bar{m}_{1})=-i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{w}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}Y+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{x}_{\alpha}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}W)+G.

Then we consider the
-i\frac{\gamma}{2}\mathcal{K}_{1}(w,r)
term.
Applying the Lemma
2.1
to
\gamma\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}m_{2}
in
\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}
, we similarly compute

\displaystyle\gamma\|\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}(\bar{R}w)\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\gamma\||D|^{\frac{1}{2}}[\bar{{\mathbf{P}}},\bar{R}]w\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\gamma\||D|^{\frac{3}{4}}R\|_{BMO}\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{-\frac{1}{4}}}\lesssim_{\underline{A}}{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\gamma\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{-\frac{1}{4}}},

\displaystyle\gamma\|\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}(\bar{W}r_{\alpha})\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\||D|^{\frac{1}{2}}[\bar{{\mathbf{P}}},\bar{W}]r_{\alpha}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\gamma\||D|^{\frac{3}{4}}W\|_{BMO}\|r\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3}{4}}}\lesssim_{\underline{A}}{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\|r\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3}{4}}},

\displaystyle\gamma\|\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}(Y\bar{W}r_{\alpha})\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\gamma\|\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}(\bar{W}Y)\|_{\dot{W}^{\frac{1}{2},4}}\|r_{\alpha}\|_{L^{4}}\lesssim\gamma\||D|^{\frac{1}{2}}[\bar{P},\bar{W}]Y\|_{L^{4}}\|r_{\alpha}\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3}{4}}}\lesssim_{\underline{A}}{\underline{A}^{\sharp}}{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\|r\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3}{4}}},

\displaystyle\gamma\|\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}[\bar{W}R_{\alpha}w]\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\gamma\|\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}[Y\bar{W}R_{\alpha}w]\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim_{\underline{A}}{\underline{A}^{\sharp}}{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left(\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}}+\gamma\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{-\frac{1}{4}}}\right).

Putting these together, we get

(4.16)

\gamma\|\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}m_{2}\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim_{\underline{A}}{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left(\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{4}}}+\gamma\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{-\frac{1}{4}}}\right).

\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}m_{2}
becomes zero after applying the Littlewood-Paley projection
{\mathbf{P}}
.
We argue as before to get

-i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbf{P}}\bar{m}_{2}=-i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{w}R-(T_{\bar{x}}\bar{R}_{\alpha}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{r}_{\alpha})W)+K=-i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{w}R-T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{r}_{\alpha}W)+K,

because we have

\gamma\|{\mathbf{P}}(T_{\bar{x}}\bar{R}_{\alpha}W)\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3}{4}}}\lesssim\|\bar{x}\|_{L^{4}}\|\bar{R}_{\alpha}\|_{L^{4}}\gamma\||D|^{\frac{3}{4}}W\|_{BMO}\lesssim{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}{\underline{A}^{\sharp}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}},

using Sobolev embedding.
Finally, the source terms estimate (
4.14
) follows directly from (
4.15
), (
4.16
) and the corresponding estimates in
[
4
]
.
∎

As for the source terms
(\underline{\mathcal{G}}_{1},\underline{\mathcal{K}}_{1})
, we have the following result:

Lemma 4.8
.

The source terms
(\underline{\mathcal{G}}_{1},\underline{\mathcal{K}}_{1})
have the decomposition

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbf{P}}\underline{\mathcal{G}}_{1}=-T_{w}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}+G,

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbf{P}}\underline{\mathcal{K}}_{1}=-iT_{w}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}Y+K,

where
(G,K)
satisfy the quadratic bound (
4.14
).

Proof.

In the source term
\underline{\mathcal{G}}_{1}
, the anti-holomorphic term
T_{r_{\alpha}}\bar{Y}
is eliminated after applying the Littlewood-Paley projection
{\mathbf{P}}
.
For terms having
r_{\alpha}
or
w_{\alpha}
, they satisfy (
4.14
).
For instance, we use (
2.1
) to estimate

\|T_{r_{\alpha}}\bar{Y}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{Y})\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\||D|^{\frac{3}{4}}r\|_{L^{2}}\||D|^{\frac{1}{4}}Y\|_{BMO}\lesssim{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\|r\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3}{4}}}.

For the rest of the terms in
\underline{\mathcal{G}}_{1}
, we write

-(T_{w}((1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}+\gamma\Im{\mathbf{W}})+\Pi(w,(1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}+\gamma\Im{\mathbf{W}}))=-T_{w}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}+G,

where terms except
-T_{w}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}
are balanced, and we can put them into
G
.
For instance, using the fact that
\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}(\mathbb{R})\hookrightarrow L^{4}(\mathbb{R})
,
we can bound

\|T_{w}\Pi(\bar{Y},R_{\alpha})\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|w\|_{L^{4}}\|\Pi(\bar{Y},R_{\alpha})\|_{L^{4}}\lesssim\||D|^{\frac{1}{4}}Y\|_{BMO}\||D|^{\frac{3}{4}}R\|_{L^{4}}\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\lesssim_{A^{\sharp}}{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}}.

We also use (
2.1
) to estimate

\gamma\|T_{w}\Im{\mathbf{W}}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\gamma\||D|^{-\frac{1}{4}}w\|_{L^{2}}\||D|^{\frac{1}{4}}{\mathbf{W}}\|_{BMO}\lesssim{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\gamma\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{-\frac{1}{4}}}.

For other terms in
\underline{\mathcal{K}}_{1}
, we use (
2.12
) to write

i(T_{1-Y}T_{w}{\underline{a}}+T_{1-Y}\Pi(w,{\underline{a}})-T_{(g+{\underline{a}})w}Y-\Pi((g+{\underline{a}})w,Y))=-iT_{w}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}Y+K,

where terms except
-iT_{w}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}Y
are balanced, and they can be absorbed into
K
.
For instance, using (
3.3
) for
{\underline{a}}
as well as the Sobolev embedding
\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}(\mathbb{R})\hookrightarrow L^{4}(\mathbb{R})
, we bound

\|T_{1-Y}T_{w}{\underline{a}}\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim(1+\|Y\|_{L^{\infty}})\|w\|_{L^{4}}\||D|^{\frac{1}{2}}{\underline{a}}\|_{L^{4}}\lesssim_{A^{\sharp}}{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}}.

These give the decomposition for
({\mathbf{P}}\underline{\mathcal{G}}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\underline{\mathcal{K}}_{1})
.
∎

Putting together Lemma
4.7
, Lemma
4.8
and equations (
4.4
), one can compute the following para-material derivatives for
(w,r)
:

Lemma 4.9
.

For
(w,r)
that satisfy (
4.4
), they have the representation

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}T_{D_{t}}w=-T_{1-\bar{Y}}(r_{\alpha}+T_{w}R_{\alpha})+G_{2}=:G_{1}+G_{2}&\\
T_{D_{t}}r=-i\gamma r+iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}(x-T_{w}Y)+K_{2}=:K_{1}+K_{2},&\end{array}\right.

and likewise with
(D_{t}w,D_{t}r)
in place of
(T_{D_{t}}w,T_{D_{t}}r)
.

(G_{1},K_{1})
satisfy the linear bound

\||D|^{-\frac{1}{4}}(G_{1},K_{1})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\lesssim_{{\underline{A}^{\sharp}}}\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{4}}}+\gamma\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{-\frac{1}{4}}},

and
(G_{2},K_{2})
satisfy the quadratic bound (
4.14
).

Proof.

From Lemma
4.7
, we know that the source terms
({\mathbf{P}}\underline{\mathcal{G}}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\underline{\mathcal{K}}_{0})
can be absorbed into
(G_{2},K_{2})
.
The source terms
({\mathbf{P}}\underline{\mathcal{G}}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\underline{\mathcal{K}}_{1})
and the terms on the left-hand side of (
4.4
) belong to
(G_{1},K_{1})
.
The only exceptions are
T_{(1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}}w
and
\gamma T_{\Im{\mathbf{W}}}w
.
For these two terms. we have

\displaystyle\|T_{(1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}}w\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\bar{Y}\|_{L^{\infty}}\||D|^{\frac{3}{4}}R\|_{BMO}\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\lesssim_{A}{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}},

\displaystyle\gamma\|T_{\Im{\mathbf{W}}}w\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\gamma\||D|^{\frac{3}{4}}W\|_{BMO}\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\lesssim{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}},

so that they can be put into
G_{2}
.
We can use para-commutator (
2.11
) to reorder paraproducts freely.
For the difference between
(T_{D_{t}}w,T_{D_{t}}r)
and
(D_{t}w,D_{t}r)
,

\displaystyle\|T_{D_{t}}w-D_{t}w\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|T_{w_{\alpha}}{\underline{b}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\Pi(w_{\alpha},{\underline{b}})\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\||D|^{\frac{3}{4}}{\underline{b}}\|_{BMO}\lesssim{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}},

\displaystyle\|T_{D_{t}}r-D_{t}r\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\|T_{r_{\alpha}}{\underline{b}}\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\|\Pi(r_{\alpha},{\underline{b}})\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\|r\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3}{4}}}\||D|^{\frac{3}{4}}{\underline{b}}\|_{BMO}\lesssim{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\|r\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3}{4}}},

so that they can be added to
(G_{2},K_{2})
, and
(D_{t}w,D_{t}r)
have the same representation as
(T_{D_{t}}w,T_{D_{t}}r)
.

To estimate
(G_{1},K_{1})
, we notice that the terms

-T_{1-\bar{Y}}r_{\alpha},\quad-i\gamma r,\quad iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}x

are already balanced, and their estimates are straightforward.
For the two other terms, we use the
L^{4}
Sobolev embedding

\displaystyle\||D|^{-\frac{1}{4}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}r_{\alpha}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|1-\bar{Y}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|w\|_{L^{4}}\||D|^{\frac{3}{4}}R\|_{L^{4}}\lesssim_{\underline{A}}A^{\sharp}\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}},

\displaystyle\|i|D|^{-\frac{1}{4}}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}T_{w}Y\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\|g+{\underline{a}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|w\|_{L^{4}}\||D|^{\frac{1}{4}}Y\|_{L^{4}}\lesssim_{\underline{A}}A^{\sharp}\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}},

where we use the fact that

\||D|^{\frac{1}{4}}Y\|_{L^{4}}\lesssim\||D|^{\frac{1}{4}}{\mathbf{W}}\|_{L^{4}}\lesssim_{\underline{A}}A^{\sharp}.

Therefore, we have shown that
(G_{1},K_{1})
satisfy the linear bound.
∎

Finally, we compute the para-material derivatives of
x=T_{1-Y}w
,
\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}r
, and
u=T_{1-Y}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}w
.

Lemma 4.10
.

For
(w,r)
that satisfy (
4.4
),
T_{D_{t}}x
has the following representation

T_{D_{t}}x=-T_{1-\bar{Y}}(T_{1-Y}r_{\alpha}+T_{x}R_{\alpha})+G_{2}=:G_{1}+G_{2},

where
G_{1}
satisfies the linear bound

\||D|^{-\frac{1}{4}}G_{1}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{A^{\sharp}}\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{4}}},

and
G_{2}
satisfies the quadratic bound

\|G_{2}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{A^{\sharp}}{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{4}}}.

Similar estimates hold when
T_{D_{t}}x
is replaced by
D_{t}x
.

Proof.

Applying the para-Leibniz rule (
2.17
), we write

T_{D_{t}}x=T_{-T_{D_{t}}Y}w+T_{1-Y}T_{D_{t}}w+G_{2},

where the para-Leibniz error can be placed into
G_{2}
.
For
T_{D_{t}}Y
, we use (
3.10
), which shows that the
T_{-T_{D_{t}}Y}w
term on the right belongs to
G_{2}
.
For the
T_{1-Y}T_{D_{t}}w
term, we apply the formula of
T_{D_{t}}w
in Lemma
4.9
and para-commutator (
2.11
) to write

-T_{1-Y}T_{1-\bar{Y}}(r_{\alpha}+T_{w}R_{\alpha})+G_{2}=-T_{1-\bar{Y}}(T_{1-Y}r_{\alpha}+T_{x}R_{\alpha})+G_{2}.

The bounds for
G_{1}
,
G_{2}
as well as the representation of
D_{t}w
follow directly from Lemma
4.9
.
∎

Lemma 4.11
.

For
(w,r)
that satisfy (
4.4
), we have the following results for para-material derivatives of
\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}r
,
\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w
and
u=T_{1-Y}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}w
:

(1)

Leading term of the para-material derivative of
\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}r
:

T_{D_{t}}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}r=-i\gamma\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}r+iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}(u-T_{w}X)+K_{2}=:K_{1}+K_{2},

and likewise with
D_{t}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}r
in place of
T_{D_{t}}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}r
.

K_{1}
satisfies the linear bound

\gamma\|K_{1}\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3}{4}}}+\|K_{1}\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{5}{4}}}\lesssim_{{\underline{A}^{\sharp}}}\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{4}}}+\gamma^{2}\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{-\frac{3}{4}}},

and
K_{2}
satisfies the quadratic bound

\gamma^{2}\|K_{2}\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim_{A^{\sharp}}{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left(\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{4}}}+\gamma^{2}\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{-\frac{3}{4}}}\right).

(2)

Leading terms of the para-material derivatives of
\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w
and
u
:

\displaystyle T_{D_{t}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w=-T_{1-\bar{Y}}(r+T_{w}R)+G_{2}=:G_{1}+G_{2},

\displaystyle T_{D_{t}}u=-T_{1-\bar{Y}}(T_{1-Y}r+T_{x}R)+G_{2}=:G_{1}+G_{2},

and likewise with
(D_{t}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w,D_{t}u)
in place of
(T_{D_{t}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w,T_{D_{t}}u)
.

G_{1}
satisfies the linear bound

\gamma^{2}\|G_{1}\|_{\dot{H}^{-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|G_{1}\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3}{4}}}\lesssim_{{\underline{A}^{\sharp}}}\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{4}}}+\gamma^{2}\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{-\frac{3}{4}}},

and
G_{2}
satisfies the quadratic bound

\gamma^{2}\|G_{2}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{A^{\sharp}}{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left(\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{4}}}+\gamma^{2}\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{-\frac{3}{4}}}\right).

Proof.

(1)

We apply the anti-derivative to
T_{D_{t}}r
in Lemma
4.9
and use (
2.11
), (
2.12
) to write:

\displaystyle T_{D_{t}}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}r=

\displaystyle-i\gamma\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}r+iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}(T_{1-Y}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}w-T_{w}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}Y)+[T_{D_{t}},\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}]r

\displaystyle-i[T_{(g+{\underline{a}})(1-Y)},\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}]w+i[T_{(g+{\underline{a}})w},\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}]Y+K_{2}.

For the three commutator terms, we write

\displaystyle[T_{D_{t}},\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}]r

\displaystyle=T_{\underline{b}}r-\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}(T_{\underline{b}}r_{\alpha})=\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}(T_{{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}}r),

\displaystyle[T_{(g+{\underline{a}})(1-Y)},\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}]w

\displaystyle=T_{(g+{\underline{a}})(1-Y)}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}w-\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}(T_{(g+{\underline{a}})(1-Y)}w)=\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}(T_{[(g+{\underline{a}})(1-Y)]_{\alpha}}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}w),

\displaystyle[T_{(g+{\underline{a}})w},\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}]Y

\displaystyle=T_{(g+{\underline{a}})w}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}Y-\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}(T_{(g+{\underline{a}})w}Y)=\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}(T_{[(g+{\underline{a}})w]_{\alpha}}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}Y).

Using (
2.1
) and (
2.2
), these commutator terms may be absorbed into the error
K_{2}
.
By
(4)
of Lemma
3.9
, one can write

\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}Y=\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}(T_{1-Y}X_{\alpha})+K_{3}=X-\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}T_{Y}X_{\alpha}+K_{3},

where the error
K_{3}
satisfies

\gamma^{2}\|K_{3}\|_{\dot{W}^{\frac{1}{2},4}}\lesssim_{A^{\sharp}}{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}.

We can bound

\displaystyle\gamma^{2}\|T_{g+{\underline{a}}}T_{w}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}T_{Y}X_{\alpha}\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\gamma^{2}\|g+{\underline{a}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{-\frac{3}{4}}}\|T_{Y}X_{\alpha}\|_{BMO^{\frac{1}{4}}}\lesssim_{\underline{A}}\gamma^{2}{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{-\frac{3}{4}}},

\displaystyle\gamma^{2}\|T_{g+{\underline{a}}}T_{w}K_{3}\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\gamma^{2}\|g+{\underline{a}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|w\|_{L^{4}}\|K_{3}\|_{\dot{W}^{\frac{1}{2},4}}\lesssim_{A^{\sharp}}{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}},

so that
\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}Y
can be replaced by
X
.

For the difference between
T_{D_{t}}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}r
and
D_{t}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}r
,

\gamma^{2}\|T_{D_{t}}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}r-D_{t}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}r\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\gamma^{2}\|T_{r}{\underline{b}}\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\gamma^{2}\|\Pi(r,{\underline{b}})\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\gamma^{2}\|r\|_{\dot{H}^{-\frac{1}{4}}}\||D|^{\frac{3}{4}}{\underline{b}}\|_{BMO},

and the difference may be absorbed into
K_{2}
.
For the estimate of
K_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\gamma^{2}\|\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}r\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3}{4}}}+\gamma\|\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}r\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{5}{4}}}\lesssim\|r\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3}{4}}}+\gamma^{2}\|r\|_{\dot{H}^{-\frac{1}{4}}},

\displaystyle\gamma\|T_{g+{\underline{a}}}u\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3}{4}}}+\|T_{g+{\underline{a}}}u\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{5}{4}}}\lesssim\|g+{\underline{a}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|1-Y\|_{L^{\infty}}\left(\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}}+\gamma^{2}\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3}{4}}}\right)

\displaystyle\gamma\|T_{g+{\underline{a}}}T_{w}X\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3}{4}}}+\|T_{g+{\underline{a}}}T_{w}X\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{5}{4}}}\lesssim_{\underline{A}^{\sharp}}\|g+{\underline{a}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|w\|_{L^{4}}\left(\|X\|_{\dot{W}^{\frac{5}{4},4}}+\gamma^{2}\|X\|_{\dot{W}^{\frac{1}{4},4}}\right),

and they satisfy the linear bound.

(2)

We apply the para-Leibniz rule Lemma
2.5
to
u
:

T_{D_{t}}u=-T_{T_{D_{t}}Y}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}w+T_{1-Y}T_{D_{t}}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}w+G_{2},

where the para-Leibniz error can be placed into
G_{2}
.
For
T_{D_{t}}Y
, we use (
3.10
), which shows that the
T_{T_{D_{t}}Y}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}w
term on the right belongs to
G_{2}
.
For the
T_{1-Y}T_{D_{t}}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}w
term, we apply the anti-derivative to
T_{D_{t}}w
in Lemma
4.9
, and para-products (
2.12
) to write

T_{D_{t}}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}w=-T_{1-\bar{Y}}(r+T_{w}R)+[T_{D_{t}},\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}]w+[T_{\bar{Y}},\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}]r_{\alpha}-[T_{(1-\bar{Y})w},\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}]R_{\alpha}+G_{2}.

The three commutator terms can be rewritten as,

\displaystyle[T_{D_{t}},\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}]w=T_{\underline{b}}w-\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}(T_{\underline{b}}w_{\alpha})=\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}(T_{{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}}w),

\displaystyle[T_{\bar{Y}},\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}]r_{\alpha}=T_{\bar{Y}}r-\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}(T_{\bar{Y}}r_{\alpha})=\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}(T_{\bar{Y}_{\alpha}}r),

\displaystyle[T_{(1-\bar{Y})w},\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}]R_{\alpha}=T_{(1-\bar{Y})w}R-\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}(T_{(1-\bar{Y})w}R_{\alpha})=\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}(T_{[(1-\bar{Y})w]_{\alpha}}R).

Using (
2.1
) and (
2.2
), these commutator terms may be absorbed into the error
G_{2}
.
Applying
T_{1-Y}
to
T_{D_{t}}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}w
and using (
2.12
), we obtain the leading term of
T_{D_{t}}u
.

For the difference between
T_{D_{t}}u
and
D_{t}u
,
we have

\gamma^{2}\|T_{D_{t}}u-D_{t}u\|_{L^{2}}\leq\gamma^{2}\|T_{u_{\alpha}}{\underline{b}}\|_{L^{2}}+\gamma^{2}\|\Pi(u_{\alpha},{\underline{b}})\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\gamma^{2}\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{-\frac{3}{4}}}\||D|^{\frac{3}{4}}{\underline{b}}\|_{BMO},

so that it may be absorbed into
G_{2}
.
Finally, to estimate
G_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\gamma^{2}\|T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{1-Y}r\|_{\dot{H}^{-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{1-Y}r\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3}{4}}}\lesssim_{\underline{A}}\|r\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3}{4}}}+\gamma^{2}\|r\|_{\dot{H}^{-\frac{1}{4}}},

\displaystyle\gamma^{2}\|T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{x}R\|_{\dot{H}^{-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{x}R\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3}{4}}}\lesssim\|x\|_{L^{4}}\left(\gamma^{2}\|R\|_{\dot{W}^{-\frac{1}{4},4}}+\|R\|_{\dot{W}^{\frac{3}{4},4}}\right)\lesssim{\underline{A}^{\sharp}}\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}},

and they satisfy the linear bound.

∎

4.4.
Normal form analysis for
(\underline{\mathcal{G}}_{1},\underline{\mathcal{K}}_{1})

In this subsection, we use the paradifferential normal form corrections to remove the source terms
({\mathbf{P}}\underline{\mathcal{G}}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\underline{\mathcal{K}}_{1})
modulo balanced cubic terms in the linearized equations.
Recall that we have

\displaystyle{\mathbf{P}}\underline{\mathcal{G}}_{1}=

\displaystyle\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{Y})-{\mathbf{P}}T_{w_{\alpha}}{\underline{b}}-\Pi(w_{\alpha},{\underline{b}})-T_{w}{\mathbf{P}}((1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}-i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbf{W}})-\Pi(w,(1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}+\gamma\Im{\mathbf{W}}),

\displaystyle{\mathbf{P}}\underline{\mathcal{K}}_{1}=

\displaystyle-{\mathbf{P}}T_{r_{\alpha}}{\underline{b}}-\Pi(r_{\alpha},{\underline{b}})+i({\mathbf{P}}T_{1-Y}T_{w}{\underline{a}}+T_{1-Y}\Pi(w,{\underline{a}})-T_{(g+{\underline{a}})w}Y-\Pi((g+{\underline{a}})w,Y)).

For simplicity regarding the balanced cases, we let
\Pi={\mathbf{P}}\Pi
to include the Littlewood-Paley projection
{\mathbf{P}}
.
We will then prove the following result:

Proposition 4.12
.

Assume that
(w,r)
solve the paradifferential equation (
4.4
), then there exists a linear paradifferential normal form correction

(\tilde{w},\tilde{r})=NF(w,r)

that solves the paradifferential equations

(4.17)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}T_{D_{t}}\tilde{w}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{r}+T_{(1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}}\tilde{w}+\gamma T_{\Im{\mathbf{W}}}\tilde{w}=-{\mathbf{P}}\underline{\mathcal{G}}_{1}(w,r)+G&\\
T_{D_{t}}\tilde{r}+i\gamma\tilde{r}-iT_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\tilde{w}=-{\mathbf{P}}\underline{\mathcal{K}}_{1}(w,r)+K,&\end{array}\right.

with the following properties:

(1)

Quadratic correction bound:

(4.18)

\|NF(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\lesssim_{{\underline{A}}}{\underline{A}^{\sharp}}\left(\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{4}}}+\gamma^{2}\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{-\frac{3}{4}}}\right).

(2)

Secondary correction bound:

(4.19)

\|NF(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\lesssim_{\underline{A}}{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left(\|(w,r)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}+\gamma^{2}\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{-1}}\right).

(3)

Cubic error bound:

(4.20)

\|(G,K)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\lesssim_{{\underline{A}^{\sharp}}}\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}{\underline{A}^{\sharp}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left(\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{4}}}+\gamma^{2}\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{-\frac{3}{4}}}\right).

Here, the secondary correction bound will be needed in order to estimate the effect of source terms for the inhomogeneous problem (
4.4
).

As a general guideline, our paradifferential normal form corrections will be either of the two cases:

(1)

High-low interactions.

(2)

Balanced interactions with low frequency output.

Motivated by the normal form transformation (
1.10
), we define the corrections by

\displaystyle\tilde{w}:=

\displaystyle-\partial_{\alpha}(T_{w}X+\Pi(w,X))-\Pi(w_{\alpha},\bar{X})

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}\left(T_{w_{\alpha}}Z+\Pi(w_{\alpha},Z)+T_{r}X_{\alpha}+\Pi(r,X_{\alpha})+\Pi(w_{\alpha},\bar{Z})\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}\left(T_{r_{\alpha}}X+\Pi(r_{\alpha},X)+T_{w}R+\Pi(w,R)+\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{X})\right)

\displaystyle+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}\left(T_{w_{\alpha}}U+\Pi(w_{\alpha},U)+T_{\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}w}X_{\alpha}+\Pi(\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w,X_{\alpha})+\Pi(w_{\alpha},\bar{U})\right)

\displaystyle+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}\left(2T_{w}X+2\Pi(w,X)+\frac{1}{2}\Pi(w,\bar{X})\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}\left(\partial_{\alpha}(T_{r}Z+\Pi(r,Z))+\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{Z})\right)

\displaystyle+i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}\left(T_{r}X+\Pi(r,X)+T_{w}Z+\Pi(w,Z)+\Pi(w,\bar{Z})\right)

\displaystyle+i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}\left(T_{r_{\alpha}}U+\Pi(r_{\alpha},U)+T_{\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}w}R+\Pi(\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}w,R)-\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{U})\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}\left(T_{w}U+T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}X+\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w,U)-\Pi(w,\bar{U})\right),

\displaystyle\tilde{r}:=

\displaystyle-T_{r_{\alpha}}X-\Pi(r_{\alpha},X)-\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{X})

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}\left(T_{r_{\alpha}}Z+T_{r}R+\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(r,Z)+\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{Z})\right)

\displaystyle+i\frac{\gamma}{2}\left(T_{w}X+\Pi(w,X)+\Pi(w,\bar{X})\right)

\displaystyle+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}\left(T_{r_{\alpha}}U+\Pi(r_{\alpha},U)+T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}R+\Pi(\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w,R)-\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{U})\right)

\displaystyle+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}\left(T_{w}Z+\Pi(w,Z)+T_{r}X+\Pi(r,X)+\Pi(w,\bar{Z})\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}\left(T_{w}U+\Pi(w,U)+T_{\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}w}X+\Pi(\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}w,X)-\Pi(w,\bar{U})\right).

We remark that this paradifferential correction is roughly the holomorphic part of the paralinearization of the normal form transformation (
1.10
) at the quadratic level.

We first verify that
(\tilde{w},\tilde{r})
satisfy the quadratic correction bound (
4.18
).
The balanced terms are straightforward; for instance,

\gamma^{2}\|\Pi(w,X)\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\lesssim_{\underline{A}}\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\gamma^{2}\|X\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim{\underline{A}}\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}}.

For low-high terms with
T_{w}
or
\gamma^{2}T_{\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}w}
, we use

\|T_{f}G\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{4}}\||D|^{\frac{1}{4}}G\|_{L^{4}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\|G\|_{\dot{W}^{\frac{1}{4},4}}

type of estimates with Sobolev embedding, which yields an
{\underline{A}^{\sharp}}
coefficient.
For other low high terms, we use (
2.1
).

As for the secondary correction bound (
4.19
), these estimates are also straightforward; for instance,

\|T_{r_{\alpha}}X\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3}{4}}}\lesssim\|r\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|D|^{\frac{1}{4}}{\mathbf{W}}\|_{BMO}\lesssim{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\|r\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Next, we plug in the corrections to the system and compute the source terms.
In order to simplify the computation below, we consider the following cases where terms may be absorbed into
(G,K)
:

(1)

Cubic and higher order terms such that the lowest frequency variable is “fully differentiated” or “over differentiated” when combined with the vorticity
\gamma
.
The “fully differentiated” cases include for instance

(|D|^{\frac{5}{4}}W,|D|^{\frac{3}{4}}R)\in BMO,\quad(|D|^{\frac{1}{4}}w,|D|^{\frac{3}{4}}r)\in L^{2},\quad\gamma^{2}(|D|^{-\frac{3}{4}}w,|D|^{-\frac{1}{4}}r)\in L^{2}.

When there are even more derivatives on these terms, they are “over differentiated”.

(2)

Cubic and higher order terms such that the lowest frequency variable is
\gamma{\underline{a}}
.
For instance, we can estimate a typical term
\gamma^{3}T_{T_{\underline{a}}x}X
in
\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}
using Sobolev embedding by

\gamma^{3}\|T_{T_{\underline{a}}x}X\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\lesssim\gamma\|{\underline{a}}\|_{L^{4}}\|w\|_{L^{4}}\gamma^{2}\||D|^{\frac{1}{4}}X\|_{BMO}\lesssim_{{\underline{A}^{\sharp}}}{\underline{A}}^{2}_{\frac{1}{4}}\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}}.

(3)

Cubic and higher order terms such that the lowest frequency variable is either
w
,
\gamma r
or
\gamma^{2}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w
.
Then we need to bound
w
,
\gamma r
or
\gamma^{2}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w
in
L^{4}
norm, and they are further bounded by

\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}}
,
\gamma\|r\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}}
or
\gamma^{2}\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{-\frac{3}{4}}}
using Sobolev embedding.
This will also bring an
{\underline{A}^{\sharp}_{\frac{1}{4}}}
constant.
For instance, we can estimate a typical term
-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{R_{\alpha}}T_{r}X_{\alpha}
in
\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}
by

-\frac{\gamma}{2g}\|T_{R_{\alpha}}T_{r}X_{\alpha}\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\lesssim\|R_{\alpha}\|_{L^{4}}\gamma\|r\|_{L^{4}}\||D|^{\frac{1}{4}}X_{\alpha}\|_{BMO}\lesssim\gamma{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}{\underline{A}^{\sharp}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\|r\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{4}}}

using Sobolev embedding.

Proof.

We insert
(\tilde{w},\tilde{r})
into the left-hand side of (
4.17
).
We consider separately the high-low corrections, frequency-balanced corrections with holomorphic variables, and the frequency-balanced corrections with anti-holomorphic variables for both equations.
For each case, we will compute the contributions of the corrections according to the power of the vorticity
\gamma
.

When computing the para-material derivatives of paraproducts, we apply the para-Leibniz rule Lemma
2.5
to distribute para-material derivatives to each variable.
In particular, when
w
is at low frequency in the first equation or
\gamma r
is at low frequency in the second equation, we apply Lemma
2.5
in the case
\sigma=0.

For the expressions of material derivatives and para-material derivatives of each variable, we use the leading terms plus errors in
BMO
or
L^{2}
based spaces in corresponding lemmas in Section
3.2
and Section
4.3
.
The only exceptions are
-\partial_{\alpha}T_{w}T_{D_{t}}X
,
-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{r}T_{D_{t}}X_{\alpha}
,
i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}T_{D_{t}}X_{\alpha}
,
-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{r}T_{D_{t}}R
and
i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}T_{D_{t}}R
.
For these terms, we need either full expressions of para-material derivatives instead of just the leading terms, or use the leading terms but applying
L^{4}
based estimates for the errors.
We also use the para-products rule
(
2.12
) to simplify some terms.

(1)
We begin with the high-low corrections in
(\tilde{w},\tilde{r})
for the first equation.
Consider first the contributions of non-vorticity terms.

\displaystyle-T_{D_{t}}\partial_{\alpha}T_{w}X=-\partial_{\alpha}T_{D_{t}}T_{w}X+T_{{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}T_{w}X

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\partial_{\alpha}T_{D_{t}w}X-\partial_{\alpha}T_{w}T_{D_{t}}X+T_{{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}}T_{w}X_{\alpha}+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{\alpha}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}(r_{\alpha}+T_{w}R_{\alpha})}X-\partial_{\alpha}T_{w}(T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}}X-{\mathbf{P}}[(1-\bar{Y})R]-\Pi(X_{\alpha},{\underline{b}}))

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma}{2}\partial_{\alpha}T_{w}({\mathbf{P}}[(1-\bar{Y})W]-X)+T_{w}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}+T_{1-Y}\bar{R}_{\alpha}}X_{\alpha}-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{w}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{W}}-T_{1-Y}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}}X_{\alpha}+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{\alpha}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}r_{\alpha}}X+\partial_{\alpha}T_{w}{\mathbf{P}}\left[(1-\bar{Y})R-i\frac{\gamma}{2}(1-\bar{Y})W\right]+T_{w}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X_{\alpha},{\underline{b}})+i\frac{\gamma}{2}\partial_{\alpha}T_{w}X

\displaystyle+T_{w}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}+T_{1-Y}\bar{R}_{\alpha}}X_{\alpha}-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{w}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{W}}-T_{1-Y}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}}X_{\alpha}+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{\alpha}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}r_{\alpha}}X+{\mathbf{P}}T_{w_{\alpha}}{\underline{b}}+T_{w}{\mathbf{P}}\left[(1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}-i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbf{W}}\right]+i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{w}{\mathbf{P}}\partial_{\alpha}(\bar{Y}W)-T_{w}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{Y}_{\alpha}R)

\displaystyle+T_{w}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X_{\alpha},{\underline{b}})+i\frac{\gamma}{2}\partial_{\alpha}T_{w}X+T_{w}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}+T_{1-Y}\bar{R}_{\alpha}}X_{\alpha}-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{w}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{W}}-T_{1-Y}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}}X_{\alpha}+G,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{\alpha}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}r_{\alpha}}X+{\mathbf{P}}T_{w_{\alpha}}{\underline{b}}+T_{w}{\mathbf{P}}\left[(1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}-i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbf{W}}\right]+i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{w}{\mathbf{P}}\partial_{\alpha}(\bar{Y}W)

\displaystyle+i\frac{\gamma}{2}\partial_{\alpha}T_{w}X+T_{w}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}}X_{\alpha}-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{w}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{W}}-T_{1-Y}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}}X_{\alpha}+G.

Here for the last four lines, we use the fact that

T_{w_{\alpha}}{\mathbf{P}}[(1-\bar{Y})R-i\frac{\gamma}{2}(1-\bar{Y})W]={\mathbf{P}}T_{w_{\alpha}}{\underline{b}}.

In a similar manner, for other non-vorticity terms, we compute

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}T_{r_{\alpha}}X

\displaystyle=-\partial_{\alpha}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}r_{\alpha}}X+G,

\displaystyle-T_{(1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}T_{w}X

\displaystyle=-T_{w}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}}X_{\alpha}+G.

For the contributions of corrections with
\gamma
coefficient, we have

\displaystyle-\gamma T_{\Im{\mathbf{W}}}\partial_{\alpha}T_{w}X=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{w}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{W}}-T_{1-Y}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}}X_{\alpha}+G,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{D_{t}}T_{w_{\alpha}}Z=

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{\partial_{\alpha}T_{D_{t}}w}Z-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{w_{\alpha}}T_{D_{t}}Z+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}(r_{\alpha\alpha}+T_{w}R_{\alpha\alpha})}Z-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{w_{\alpha}}X+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{w_{\alpha}}Z+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{r_{\alpha\alpha}}Z-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{w_{\alpha}}X+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{w_{\alpha}}Z+G,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{D_{t}}T_{r}X_{\alpha}=

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{T_{D_{t}}r}X_{\alpha}-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{r}T_{D_{t}}X_{\alpha}+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{r}X_{\alpha}-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{w}X_{\alpha}+\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{r}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}+G,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{D_{t}}T_{r_{\alpha}}X=

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{\partial_{\alpha}T_{D_{t}}r}X-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{r_{\alpha}}T_{D_{t}}X+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{r_{\alpha}}X-i\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{T_{g+{\underline{a}}}(x_{\alpha}-T_{w}Y_{\alpha})}X+\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{r_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{r_{\alpha}}X-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{w_{\alpha}}X+\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{r_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R+G,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{D_{t}}T_{w}R=

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{D_{t}w}R-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{w}T_{D_{t}}R

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}r_{\alpha}+T_{w}R_{\alpha}}R-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{w}(iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}Y-i\gamma R)+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{r_{\alpha}}R-i\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{w}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}Y+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{w}R+G,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}(T_{r_{\alpha}}Z+T_{r}R)=

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{r_{\alpha\alpha}}Z-\frac{\gamma}{g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{r_{\alpha}}R-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{r}T_{|1-Y|^{2}}R_{\alpha}+G,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}T_{w}X=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{w_{\alpha}}X+i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{w}T_{|1-Y|^{2}}{\mathbf{W}}+G.

The other
\gamma
contributions can be absorbed into
G
.

As for the contributions of corrections with
\gamma^{2}
coefficient, we compute

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{D_{t}}T_{w_{\alpha}}U=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{\partial_{\alpha}T_{D_{t}}w}U+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{w_{\alpha}}T_{D_{t}}U+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}r_{\alpha\alpha}}U-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{w_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}Z+G,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{D_{t}}T_{\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}w}X_{\alpha}=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{T_{D_{t}}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}w}X_{\alpha}+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}w}T_{D_{t}}X_{\alpha}+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}r}X_{\alpha}-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}w}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}+G,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{g}T_{D_{t}}T_{w}X=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{g}T_{D_{t}w}X+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{g}T_{w}T_{D_{t}}X+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{g}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}r_{\alpha}}X-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{g}T_{w}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R+G,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}T_{D_{t}}T_{r_{\alpha}}Z=

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}T_{\partial_{\alpha}T_{D_{t}}r}Z-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}T_{r_{\alpha}}T_{D_{t}}Z+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}T_{-i\gamma r_{\alpha}+iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}(x_{\alpha}-T_{w}Y_{\alpha})}Z-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}T_{r_{\alpha}}(igX-i\gamma Z)+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{2g^{2}}T_{r_{\alpha}}Z-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{w_{\alpha}}Z-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{r_{\alpha}}X+G,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}T_{D_{t}}T_{r}Z_{\alpha}=

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}T_{T_{D_{t}}r}Z_{\alpha}-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}T_{r}T_{D_{t}}Z_{\alpha}+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}T_{-i\gamma r+iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}(x-T_{w}Y)}R-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}T_{r}(igT_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}-i\gamma R)+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{2g^{2}}T_{r}R-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{w}R-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{r}T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}+G.

Here we use (
3.12
) to change
Z_{\alpha}
into
R
.

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}T_{r_{\alpha}}U=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}r_{\alpha\alpha}}U+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{r_{\alpha}}X+G,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}R=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{w}R+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}+G,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}T_{w}Z=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{w_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}Z+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{w}R+G,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}T_{r}X=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{r_{\alpha}}X+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{r}T_{|1-Y|^{2}}{\mathbf{W}}+G.

The other
\gamma^{2}
terms have “over differentiated” lowest frequency variables, and may be absorbed into
G
.

For the contributions of corrections with
\gamma^{3}
coefficient,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{D_{t}}T_{r}X=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{T_{D_{t}}r}X+i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{r}T_{D_{t}}X+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{-i\gamma r+iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}(x-T_{w}Y)}X-i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{r}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}T_{r}X-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{w}X-i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{r}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R+G,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{D_{t}}T_{w}Z=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{D_{t}w}Z+i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{w}T_{D_{t}}Z+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}(r_{\alpha}+T_{w}R_{\alpha})}Z+i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{w}(igX-i\gamma Z)+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{r_{\alpha}}Z-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{w}X+\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}T_{w}Z+G,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{D_{t}}T_{r_{\alpha}}U=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{T_{D_{t}}r_{\alpha}}U+i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{r_{\alpha}}T_{D_{t}}U+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{-i\gamma r_{\alpha}+iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}(x_{\alpha}-T_{w}Y_{\alpha})}U-i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{r_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}Z

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}T_{r_{\alpha}}U-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{w_{\alpha}}U-i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{r_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}Z,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{D_{t}}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}R=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{T_{D_{t}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}R+i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}T_{D_{t}}R+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}(r+T_{w}R)}R+i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}(iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}Y-i\gamma R)+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}r}R-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}Y+\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}R+G,

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}T_{w}U=

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{w_{\alpha}}U+\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{w}X+G.

Here we changed
U_{\alpha}
to
X
according to (
3.13
).
The other
\gamma^{3}
terms have “over differentiated” lowest frequency variables, and we put them into
G
.

For the contributions of corrections with
\gamma^{4}
coefficient,

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}T_{D_{t}}T_{w}U=

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}T_{D_{t}w}U+\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}T_{w}T_{D_{t}}U+G

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}(r_{\alpha}+T_{w}R_{\alpha})}U-\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}T_{w}T_{1-\bar{Y}}Z+G

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}r_{\alpha}}U-\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}T_{w}T_{1-\bar{Y}}Z+G,

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}T_{D_{t}}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}X

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}T_{T_{D_{t}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}X+\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}T_{D_{t}}X+G

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}r}X-\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R+G.

The rest of correction terms with
\gamma^{4}
or
\gamma^{5}
coefficient are perturbative.

Summing all of above contributions, we obtain that the total correction is

{\mathbf{P}}T_{w_{\alpha}}{\underline{b}}+T_{w}{\mathbf{P}}\left((1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}-i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbf{W}}\right)+G.

(2)
Continuing with the contributions for the frequency balanced corrections with holomorphic variables in the first equation.
The computation is similar to case
(1)
in the sense that we simply replace the unbalanced par-Leibniz error estimate by the corresponding balanced para-Leibniz estimate in Lemma
2.5
.
As a result, we get the total correction is given by

\Pi\left(w_{\alpha},{\mathbf{P}}{\underline{b}}\right)+\Pi\left(w,(1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}-i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbf{W}}\right)+G.

(3)
Continuing with the frequency balanced corrections with one anti-holomorphic variable.
The computation is similar to case
(2)
.

\displaystyle-T_{D_{t}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},\bar{X})

\displaystyle=-\Pi(\partial_{\alpha}T_{D_{t}}w,\bar{X})-\Pi(w_{\alpha},T_{D_{t}}\bar{X})+G

\displaystyle=\Pi(r_{\alpha\alpha},\bar{X})+\Pi(w_{\alpha},T_{1-Y}\bar{R})+G,

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{X})

\displaystyle=-T_{1-\bar{Y}}\Pi(r_{\alpha\alpha},\bar{X})-\Pi(r_{\alpha},T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{X}_{\alpha})+G

\displaystyle=-T_{1-\bar{Y}}\Pi(r_{\alpha\alpha},\bar{X})-\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{Y})+G.

For the last term we use (
3.11
) to replace
T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{X}_{\alpha}
by
\bar{Y}
.
Then we have

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{D_{t}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},\bar{Z})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma}{2g}\Pi(\partial_{\alpha}T_{D_{t}}w,\bar{Z})-\frac{\gamma}{2g}\Pi(w_{\alpha},T_{D_{t}}\bar{Z})+G

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma}{2g}\Pi(r_{\alpha\alpha}+T_{w}R_{\alpha\alpha},\bar{Z})-\frac{\gamma}{2g}\Pi(w_{\alpha},-ig\bar{X}+i\gamma\bar{Z})+G

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma}{2g}\Pi(r_{\alpha\alpha},\bar{Z})+i\frac{\gamma}{2}\Pi(w_{\alpha},\bar{X})-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}\Pi(w_{\alpha},\bar{Z})+G,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{D_{t}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{X})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma}{2g}\Pi(\partial_{\alpha}T_{D_{t}}r,\bar{X})-\frac{\gamma}{2g}\Pi(r_{\alpha},T_{D_{t}}\bar{X})+G

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma}{2g}\Pi(-i\gamma r_{\alpha}+iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}(x_{\alpha}-T_{w}Y_{\alpha}),\bar{X})+\frac{\gamma}{2g}\Pi(r_{\alpha},T_{1-Y}\bar{R})+G

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{X})-i\frac{\gamma}{2}\Pi(w_{\alpha},\bar{X})+\frac{\gamma}{2g}\Pi(r_{\alpha},T_{1-Y}\bar{R})+G,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{Z})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\Pi(r_{\alpha\alpha},\bar{Z})-\frac{\gamma}{2g}\Pi(r_{\alpha},T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{R})+G,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(w,\bar{X})

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma}{2}\Pi(w_{\alpha},T_{1-Y}\bar{X})+i\frac{\gamma}{2}\Pi(w,T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{X}_{\alpha})+G.

For the first term of the last contribution, and the second term of the first contribution, we can combine them to get

\Pi\left(w_{\alpha},T_{1-Y}\bar{R}+i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{1-Y}\bar{X}\right)=\Pi\left(w_{\alpha},\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}\left((1-Y)\bar{R}+i\frac{\gamma}{2}(1-Y)\bar{W}\right)\right)+G=\Pi(w_{\alpha},\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}{\underline{b}})+G,

which is the other part of the desired balanced truncation
\Pi(w_{\alpha},{\underline{b}})
in
{\mathbf{P}}\underline{\mathcal{G}}_{1}
.

For the contributions of
\gamma^{2}
term, they are

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{D_{t}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},\bar{U})

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}\Pi(\partial_{\alpha}T_{D_{t}}w,\bar{U})-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}\Pi(w_{\alpha},T_{D_{t}}\bar{U})+G

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}\Pi(r_{\alpha\alpha}+T_{w}R_{\alpha\alpha},\bar{U})+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}\Pi(w_{\alpha},T_{1-Y}\bar{Z})+G

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}\Pi(r_{\alpha\alpha},\bar{U})+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}\Pi(w_{\alpha},T_{1-Y}\bar{Z})+G,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{D_{t}}\Pi(w,\bar{X})

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}\Pi(T_{D_{t}}w,\bar{X})+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}\Pi(w,T_{D_{t}}\bar{X})+G

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}\Pi(r_{\alpha}+T_{w}R_{\alpha},\bar{X})-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}\Pi(w,T_{1-Y}\bar{R})+G

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{X})-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}\Pi(w,T_{1-Y}\bar{R})+G,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}T_{D_{t}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{Z})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}\Pi(\partial_{\alpha}T_{D_{t}}r,\bar{Z})-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},T_{D_{t}}\bar{Z})+G

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}\Pi(-i\gamma r_{\alpha}+iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}(x_{\alpha}-T_{w}Y_{\alpha}),\bar{Z})-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},-ig\bar{X}+i\gamma\bar{Z})+G

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}\Pi(w_{\alpha},\bar{Z})+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{X})+G,

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{U})

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\Pi(r_{\alpha\alpha},\bar{U})-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}\Pi(r_{\alpha},T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{X})+G,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(w,\bar{Z})

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},\bar{Z})+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}\Pi(w,T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{R})+G.

The rest of the contributions are given by

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{D_{t}}\Pi(w,\bar{Z})

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}\Pi(T_{D_{t}}w,\bar{Z})+i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}\Pi(w,T_{D_{t}}\bar{Z})+G

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}\Pi(r_{\alpha}+T_{w}R_{\alpha},\bar{Z})+i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}\Pi(w,-ig\bar{X}+i\gamma\bar{Z})+G

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{Z})+\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}\Pi(w,\bar{X})-\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}\Pi(w,\bar{Z})+G,

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{D_{t}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{U})

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}\Pi(\partial_{\alpha}T_{D_{t}}r,\bar{U})-i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},T_{D_{t}}\bar{U})+G

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}\Pi(-i\gamma r_{\alpha}+iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}(x_{\alpha}-T_{w}Y_{\alpha}),\bar{U})+i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},T_{1-Y}\bar{Z})+G

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{U})+\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}\Pi(w_{\alpha},\bar{U})+i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},T_{1-Y}\bar{Z})+G,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}T_{D_{t}}\Pi(w,\bar{U})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}\Pi(T_{D_{t}}w,\bar{U})-\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}\Pi(w,T_{D_{t}}\bar{U})+G

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{U})+\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}\Pi(w,T_{1-Y}\bar{Z})+G,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(w,\bar{U})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},\bar{U})-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}\Pi(w,T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{X})+G.

The other contributions have full or over differentiated variables at low frequency, and can be put into
G
.
Collecting all the corrections above, the total frequency balanced corrections with one anti-holomorphic variable are given by

-\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{Y})+\Pi(w_{\alpha},\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}{\underline{b}})+\Pi\left(w,i\frac{\gamma}{2}\bar{W}\right)+G.

Adding the contributions of corrections in cases
(1)
,
(2)
,
(3)
, the sum is exactly
-{\mathbf{P}}\underline{\mathcal{G}}_{1}+G
.

(4)
Next, we compute the second equation in (
4.17
).
We begin with the high-low corrections.
Consider first the contributions of non-vorticity terms,

\displaystyle-T_{D_{t}}T_{r_{\alpha}}X=

\displaystyle-T_{\partial_{\alpha}T_{D_{t}}r}X-T_{r_{\alpha}}T_{D_{t}}X+K

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle iT_{\gamma r_{\alpha}-T_{g+{\underline{a}}}(x_{\alpha}-T_{w}Y_{\alpha})}X+T_{r_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R+K

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\gamma T_{r_{\alpha}}X-iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}T_{((1-Y)w)_{\alpha}}X+T_{r_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R+K,

\displaystyle iT_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\partial_{\alpha}T_{w}X=

\displaystyle iT_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}T_{((1-Y)w)_{\alpha}}W+iT_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}T_{w}T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}+K

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}T_{((1-Y)w)_{\alpha}}X+K.

Then for the contributions of the
\gamma
terms, they are
-i\gamma T_{r_{\alpha}}X
and

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{D_{t}}T_{r_{\alpha}}Z

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{\partial_{\alpha}T_{D_{t}}r}Z-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{r_{\alpha}}T_{D_{t}}Z+K

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{\gamma r_{\alpha}-T_{g+{\underline{a}}}(x_{\alpha}-T_{w}Y_{\alpha})}Z-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{r_{\alpha}}(igX-i\gamma Z)+K

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{g}T_{r_{\alpha}}Z-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{w_{\alpha}}Z-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{r_{\alpha}}X+K,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{D_{t}}T_{r}R

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{D_{t}r}R-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{r}T_{D_{t}}R+K

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{\gamma r-T_{g+{\underline{a}}}(x-T_{w}Y)}R-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{r}(igT_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}-i\gamma R)+K

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{g}T_{r}R-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{w}R-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{r}T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}+K,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{D_{t}}T_{w}X

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{D_{t}w}X+i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{w}T_{D_{t}}X+K

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}(r_{\alpha}+T_{w}R_{\alpha})}X-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{w}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R+K

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}r_{\alpha}}X-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{w}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R+K,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}T_{w_{\alpha}}Z=i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{w_{\alpha}}Z

\displaystyle+K,\quad i\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}T_{r}X_{\alpha}=i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{r}T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}+K,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}T_{r_{\alpha}}X=i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{r_{\alpha}}X

\displaystyle+K,\quad i\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}T_{w}R=i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{w}R+K.

Next, for the contributions of the
\gamma^{2}
terms, they are given by

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{r_{\alpha}}Z

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{r}R-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2}T_{w}X,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{D_{t}}T_{r_{\alpha}}U

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{T_{D_{t}}r_{\alpha}}U+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{r_{\alpha}}T_{D_{t}}U+K

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{-i\gamma r_{\alpha}+iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}(x_{\alpha}-T_{w}Y_{\alpha})}U-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{r_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}Z+K

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma^{3}}{2g}T_{r_{\alpha}}U-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2}T_{T_{1-Y}w_{\alpha}}U-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{r_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}Z+K,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{D_{t}}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}R

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{T_{D_{t}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}R+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}T_{D_{t}}R+K

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}(r+T_{w}R)}R+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}(iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}Y-i\gamma R)+K

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}r}R-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}Y+\frac{\gamma^{3}}{2g}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}R+K,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{D_{t}}T_{w}Z

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{D_{t}w}Z+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{w}T_{D_{t}}Z+K

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}(r_{\alpha}+T_{w}R_{\alpha})}Z+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{w}(igX-i\gamma Z)+K

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}r_{\alpha}}Z-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}T_{w}X+\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{w}Z+K,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{D_{t}}T_{r}X

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{D_{t}r}X+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{r}T_{D_{t}}X+K

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{-i\gamma r+iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}(x-T_{w}Y)}X-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{r}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R+K

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{r}X-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}T_{w}X-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{r}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R+K,

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}T_{w_{\alpha}}U=

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2}T_{1-Y}T_{w_{\alpha}}U+K,\quad\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}X_{\alpha}=\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}Y+K,

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{2}}{g}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}T_{w}X=

\displaystyle\gamma^{2}T_{1-Y}T_{w}X+K,\quad i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}T_{r_{\alpha}}Z=i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{1-Y}T_{r_{\alpha}}Z+K,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}T_{r}Z_{\alpha}=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{1-Y}T_{r}R+K.

Continuing with the contributions of the
\gamma^{3}
corrections, the first few terms are

-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{2g}T_{r_{\alpha}}U-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{2g}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}R-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{w}Z-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{r}X.

The contributions of other
\gamma^{3}
terms are given by

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{D_{t}}T_{w}U

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{D_{t}w}U+\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{w}T_{D_{t}}U+K

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}(r_{\alpha}+T_{w}R_{\alpha})}U-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{w}T_{1-\bar{Y}}Z+K

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}r_{\alpha}}U-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{w}T_{1-\bar{Y}}Z+K,

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{D_{t}}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}X

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{T_{D_{t}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}X+\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}T_{D_{t}}X+K

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}(r+T_{w}R)}X-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R+K

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}r}X-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R+K,

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}T_{r}X=

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{1-Y}T_{r}X+K,\quad\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}T_{w}Z=\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{1-Y}T_{w}Z+K,

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}T_{r_{\alpha}}U=

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{1-Y}T_{r_{\alpha}}U+K,\quad\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}R=\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{1-Y}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}R+K.

For the contributions of corrections with
\gamma^{4}
coefficient, they are given by

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g}T_{w}U+i\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}X,

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}(T_{w}U+\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}X)

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g}T_{w}U-i\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}w}X+K.

Adding all the contributions above, we get the total correction

{\mathbf{P}}T_{r_{\alpha}}{\underline{b}}-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{1-Y}T_{w}R+T_{(g+{\underline{a}})w}Y+K.

(5)
Continuing with the contributions for the frequency balanced corrections with holomorphic variables for the second equation of (
4.17
).
The computation is similar to case
(4)
in the sense that we simply replace the unbalanced par-Leibniz error estimates by the corresponding balanced para-Leibniz estimates in Lemma
2.5
.
Consequently, the total correction is given by

\Pi\left(r_{\alpha},{\mathbf{P}}(R(1-\bar{Y}))-i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbf{P}}(W(1-\bar{Y}))\right)-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{1-Y}\Pi(w,R)+\Pi((g+{\underline{a}})w,Y)+K.

(6)
Finally, we compute the frequency balanced corrections with one anti-holomorphic variable for the second equation of (
4.17
).

\displaystyle-T_{D_{t}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{X})

\displaystyle=-\Pi(\partial_{\alpha}T_{D_{t}}r,\bar{X})-\Pi(r_{\alpha},T_{D_{t}}\bar{X})+K

\displaystyle=-\Pi(-i\gamma r_{\alpha}+iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}(x_{\alpha}-T_{w}Y_{\alpha}),\bar{X})+\Pi(r_{\alpha},T_{1-Y}\bar{R})+K

\displaystyle=i\gamma\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{X})-ig\Pi(T_{1-Y}w_{\alpha},\bar{X})+\Pi(r_{\alpha},T_{1-Y}\bar{R})+K,

\displaystyle iT_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},\bar{X})

\displaystyle=ig\Pi(T_{1-Y}w_{\alpha},\bar{X})+K,\quad-i\gamma\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{X}),

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{D_{t}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{Z})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma}{2g}\Pi(\partial_{\alpha}T_{D_{t}}r,\bar{Z})-\frac{\gamma}{2g}\Pi(r_{\alpha},T_{D_{t}}\bar{Z})+K

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma}{2g}\Pi(-i\gamma r_{\alpha}+iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}(x_{\alpha}-T_{w}Y_{\alpha}),\bar{Z})-\frac{\gamma}{2g}\Pi(r_{\alpha},-ig\bar{X}+i\gamma\bar{Z})+K

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma}{2}\Pi(T_{1-Y}w_{\alpha},\bar{Z})+i\frac{\gamma}{2}\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{X})+K,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{D_{t}}\Pi(w,\bar{X})

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma}{2}\Pi(T_{D_{t}}w,\bar{X})+i\frac{\gamma}{2}\Pi(w,T_{D_{t}}\bar{X})+K

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma}{2}\Pi(T_{1-\bar{Y}}(r_{\alpha}+T_{w}R_{\alpha}),\bar{X})-i\frac{\gamma}{2}\Pi(w,T_{1-Y}\bar{R})+K

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma}{2}\Pi(T_{1-\bar{Y}}r_{\alpha},\bar{X})-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{1-Y}\Pi(w,\bar{R})+K,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},\bar{Z})

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma}{2}\Pi(T_{1-Y}w_{\alpha},\bar{Z})+K,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{X})

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma}{2}\Pi(r_{\alpha},T_{1-Y}\bar{X})+K.

The contributions of
\gamma^{2}
correction terms are given by

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{Z})-

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2}\Pi(w,\bar{X}),

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{D_{t}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{U})

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}\Pi(\partial_{\alpha}T_{D_{t}}r,\bar{U})-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}\Pi(r_{\alpha},T_{D_{t}}\bar{U})+K

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}\Pi(-i\gamma r_{\alpha}+iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}(x_{\alpha}-T_{w}Y_{\alpha}),\bar{U})+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}\Pi(r_{\alpha},T_{1-Y}\bar{Z})+K

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{2g}\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{U})+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2}\Pi(T_{1-Y}w_{\alpha},\bar{U})+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}\Pi(r_{\alpha},T_{1-Y}\bar{Z})+K,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{D_{t}}\Pi(w,\bar{Z})

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}\Pi(T_{D_{t}}w,\bar{Z})+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}\Pi(w,T_{D_{t}}\bar{Z})+K

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}\Pi(T_{1-\bar{Y}}(r_{\alpha}+T_{w}R_{\alpha}),\bar{Z})+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}\Pi(w,-ig\bar{X}+i\gamma\bar{Z})+K

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}\Pi(T_{1-\bar{Y}}r_{\alpha},\bar{Z})+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}\Pi(w,\bar{X})-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}\Pi(w,\bar{Z})+K,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},\bar{U})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2}\Pi(T_{1-Y}w_{\alpha},\bar{U})+K,

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\Pi(w,\bar{X})

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}\Pi(T_{1-Y}w,\bar{X})+K,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{Z})

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}\Pi(T_{1-Y}r_{\alpha},\bar{Z})+K.

Continuing with the contributions of
\gamma^{3}
and
\gamma^{4}
terms, they are

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{3}}{2g}\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{U})-

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}\Pi(w,\bar{Z})-i\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g}\Pi(w,\bar{U}),

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{D_{t}}\Pi(w,\bar{U})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}\Pi(T_{D_{t}}w,\bar{U})-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}\Pi(w,T_{D_{t}}\bar{U})+K

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}\Pi(T_{1-\bar{Y}}(r_{\alpha}+T_{w}R_{\alpha}),\bar{U})+\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}\Pi(w,T_{1-Y}\bar{Z})+K

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}\Pi(T_{1-\bar{Y}}r_{\alpha},\bar{U})+\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}\Pi(w,T_{1-Y}\bar{Z})+K,

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\Pi(w,\bar{Z})

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}\Pi(T_{1-Y}w,\bar{Z})+K,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{U})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}\Pi(T_{1-Y}r_{\alpha},\bar{U})+K,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\Pi(w,\bar{U})

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g}\Pi(T_{1-Y}w,\bar{U})+K.

Summing all the contributions above and using the definitions of
{\underline{a}}
and
{\underline{b}}
, we get that the total correction in this case is given by

\Pi\left(r_{\alpha},T_{1-Y}\bar{R}+i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{1-Y}\bar{X}\right)-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{1-Y}\Pi(w,\bar{R})+K=\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}{\underline{b}})-iT_{1-Y}\Pi(w,\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}{\underline{a}})+K.

Collecting the contributions of corrections in cases
(4)
,
(5)
,
(6)
together, the sum is exactly
-{\mathbf{P}}\underline{\mathcal{K}}_{1}+K
.
∎

4.5.
Normal form analysis for
(\underline{\mathcal{G}}_{0},\underline{\mathcal{K}}_{0})

In this subsection, we use the paradifferential normal form corrections to remove the main part of the source terms
({\mathbf{P}}\underline{\mathcal{G}}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\underline{\mathcal{K}}_{0})
in the linearized equations.

For simplicity regarding the balanced cases, we let
\Pi={\mathbf{P}}\Pi
to include the Littlewood-Paley projection
{\mathbf{P}}
.
We will then prove the following result:

Proposition 4.13
.

Assume that
(w,r)
solve the paradifferential equations (
4.4
), then there exists a linear paradifferential normal form correction

(\tilde{w},\tilde{r})=NF(w,r)

that solves the paradifferential equations

(4.21)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}T_{D_{t}}\tilde{w}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{r}+T_{(1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}}\tilde{w}+\gamma T_{\Im{\mathbf{W}}}\tilde{w}=-{\mathbf{P}}\underline{\mathcal{G}}_{0}(w,r)+G&\\
T_{D_{t}}\tilde{r}+i\gamma\tilde{r}-iT_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\tilde{w}=-{\mathbf{P}}\underline{\mathcal{K}}_{0}(w,r)+K,&\end{array}\right.

with the following properties:

(1)

Quadratic correction bound:

\|NF(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\lesssim_{{\underline{A}}}{\underline{A}^{\sharp}}\left(\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{4}}}+\gamma^{2}\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{-\frac{3}{4}}}\right).

(2)

Secondary correction bound:

\|NF(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\lesssim_{\underline{A}}{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left(\|(w,r)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}+\gamma^{2}\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{-1}}\right).

(3)

Cubic error bound:

\|(G,K)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\lesssim_{{\underline{A}^{\sharp}}}\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}{\underline{A}^{\sharp}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\left(\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{4}}}+\gamma^{2}\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{-\frac{3}{4}}}\right).

The idea to construct the paradifferential corrections here is similar to that for
({\mathbf{P}}\underline{\mathcal{G}}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\underline{\mathcal{K}}_{1})
corrections in Section
4.4
.
Inspired by the normal form transformation (
1.10
), we define the corrections
(\tilde{w},\tilde{r})
by

\displaystyle\tilde{w}=

\displaystyle-{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}{\mathbf{W}})-\frac{\gamma}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}{\mathbf{W}})-\frac{\gamma}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}{\mathbf{W}})+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}W)
