\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)+i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}W)-i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)-\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}W),

\displaystyle\tilde{r}=

\displaystyle-{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)-\frac{\gamma}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)+i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}W)-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)

\displaystyle+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}W)-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}W).

We remark that this paradifferential correction is roughly the anti-holomorphic part of the linearization of the normal form transformation (
1.10
) at the quadratic level.

These correction bounds are easily verified and are similar to the estimates in Section
4.4
using Sobolev embeddings.
Lemma
4.7
shows that the main part of
({\mathbf{P}}\underline{\mathcal{G}}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\underline{\mathcal{K}}_{0})
is
(\mathcal{G}_{0,0},\mathcal{K}_{0,0})
.
Therefore, it remains to show that these corrections eliminate
(\mathcal{G}_{0,0},\mathcal{K}_{0,0})
modulo perturbative terms.

Proof.

We follow the same strategy in Section
4.4
, and put all the balanced cubic and higher order terms into
(G,K)
.
We will consider first the contributions of corrections for the first equation of (
4.21
), then for the second equation of (
4.21
).
For each case, we again compute according to the power of the vorticity
\gamma
.
When computing the para-material derivatives of paraproducts, we apply the para-Leibniz rule Lemma
2.5
to distribute para-material derivatives to each variable.
For the expressions of material derivatives and para-material derivatives of each variable, we use the leading terms plus errors measured in either
BMO
or
L^{4}
based spaces in corresponding Lemmas in Section
3.2
and Section
4.3
.

(1)
We start with the first equation of (
4.21
).
We begin by computing the contributions of corrections of non-vorticity and
\gamma
terms.

\displaystyle-T_{D_{t}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}{\mathbf{W}})

\displaystyle=-{\mathbf{P}}(D_{t}\bar{x}{\mathbf{W}})-{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{D_{t}}{\mathbf{W}})+G

\displaystyle={\mathbf{P}}(T_{1-Y}(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{r}_{\alpha}+T_{\bar{x}}\bar{R}_{\alpha}){\mathbf{W}})+{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha})+G

\displaystyle={\mathbf{P}}(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{r}_{\alpha}T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}})+{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha})+G

\displaystyle={\mathbf{P}}(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{r}_{\alpha}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}Y)+T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R_{\alpha})+G.

Here we use (
2.16
) to move para-coefficients
T_{1-Y}
,
T_{1-\bar{Y}}
.
We also use (
3.11
) to change
T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}}
to
T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}Y
.

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)=

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}_{\alpha}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)-T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R_{\alpha})+G,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{D_{t}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}{\mathbf{W}})=

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(D_{t}\bar{r}{\mathbf{W}})-\frac{\gamma}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{D_{t}}{\mathbf{W}})+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}((i\gamma\bar{r}-iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}(\bar{x}-T_{\bar{w}}\bar{Y})){\mathbf{W}})

\displaystyle+\frac{\gamma}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha})+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}{\mathbf{W}})+i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}{\mathbf{W}})+\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R_{\alpha})+G,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{D_{t}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)=

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(D_{t}\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)-\frac{\gamma}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{D_{t}}R)-\frac{\gamma}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{D_{t}{\mathbf{W}}}R)+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(T_{1-Y}(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{r}_{\alpha}+T_{\bar{x}}\bar{R}_{\alpha})T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}(T_{g+{\underline{a}}}Y-\gamma R))+\frac{\gamma}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}}R)+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}_{\alpha}R)-i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}Y)+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)+G,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)=

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}_{\alpha}R)-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R_{\alpha})+G,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}W)=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}_{\alpha}W)+i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}{\mathbf{W}})+G.

Continuing with the contributions of corrections of
\gamma^{2}
terms, we compute

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{D_{t}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}{\mathbf{W}})=

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(T_{D_{t}}\bar{u}{\mathbf{W}})-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{D_{t}}{\mathbf{W}})+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(T_{1-Y}(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{r}+T_{\bar{x}}\bar{R}){\mathbf{W}})+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha})+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}{\mathbf{W}})+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R_{\alpha})+G,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{D_{t}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}W)=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(D_{t}\bar{x}W)+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{D_{t}}W)+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(T_{1-Y}(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{r}_{\alpha}+T_{\bar{x}}\bar{R}_{\alpha})W)-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R)+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{r}_{\alpha}T_{1-Y}W)-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)+G,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}T_{D_{t}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)=

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}(D_{t}\bar{r}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{D_{t}}R)

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{D_{t}{\mathbf{W}}}R)+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}((i\gamma\bar{r}-iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}(\bar{x}-T_{\bar{w}}\bar{Y}))T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}(iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}Y-i\gamma R))+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}}R)+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}i{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}Y)+G,

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)=

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R_{\alpha})+G,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}W)=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}_{\alpha}W)+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}{\mathbf{W}})+G.

For the contributions of corrections of
\gamma^{3}
and
\gamma^{4}
terms, we compute

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{D_{t}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}W)=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}(D_{t}\bar{r}W)+i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{D_{t}}W)+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}((i\gamma\bar{r}-iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}(\bar{x}-T_{\bar{w}}\bar{Y}))W)-i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R)+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}W)+\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}W)-i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)+G,

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{D_{t}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)=

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}(T_{D_{t}}\bar{u}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)-i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{D_{t}}R)

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{D_{t}{\mathbf{W}}}R)+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}(T_{1-Y}(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{r}+T_{\bar{x}}\bar{R})T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}(iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}Y-i\gamma R))+i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}}R)+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{r}R)+\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}Y)-\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)+G,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}W)=

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{x}W)-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{u}{\mathbf{W}})+G,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}T_{D_{t}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}W)=

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}(T_{D_{t}}\bar{u}W)-\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{D_{t}}W)+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}(T_{1-Y}(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{r}+T_{\bar{x}}\bar{R})W)+\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R)+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}W)+\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)+G.

The contributions of other corrections are perturbative and can be absorbed into
G
.
Collecting all the contributions of the corrections together, we find that the total contributions for the first equation is exactly
-\mathcal{G}_{0,0}+G
.

(2)
Then we consider the second equation of (
4.21
).
We begin by computing the contributions
of corrections of non-vorticity and
\gamma
terms.

\displaystyle-T_{D_{t}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)=

\displaystyle-{\mathbf{P}}(D_{t}\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)-{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{D_{t}{\mathbf{W}}}R)-{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{D_{t}}R)+K

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbf{P}}(T_{1-Y}(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{r}_{\alpha}+T_{\bar{x}}\bar{R}_{\alpha})T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)+{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}}R)

\displaystyle-i{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}(T_{g+{\underline{a}}}Y-\gamma R))+K

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbf{P}}(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{r}_{\alpha}R)-iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}Y)+i\gamma{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)+K,

\displaystyle iT_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}{\mathbf{W}})=

\displaystyle iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}Y)+K,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{D_{t}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)=

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(D_{t}\bar{r}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)-\frac{\gamma}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{D_{t}{\mathbf{W}}}R)-\frac{\gamma}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{D_{t}}R)+K

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}((\gamma\bar{r}-T_{g+{\underline{a}}}(\bar{x}-T_{\bar{w}}\bar{Y}))T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)+\frac{\gamma}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}}R)

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}(T_{g+{\underline{a}}}Y-\gamma R))+K

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)-i\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}Y)+K,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{D_{t}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}W)=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbf{P}}(D_{t}\bar{x}W)+i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{D_{t}}W)+K

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbf{P}}(T_{1-Y}(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{r}_{\alpha}+T_{\bar{x}}\bar{R}_{\alpha})W)-i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R)+K

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbf{P}}(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{r}_{\alpha}W)-i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)+K,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}{\mathbf{W}})=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{W}})+K=i\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}Y)+K,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)+K,\quad\text{ and }-i\gamma{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R).

Continuing with the contributions of corrections of
\gamma^{2}
terms, we compute

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{D_{t}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)=

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(T_{D_{t}}\bar{u}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{D_{t}{\mathbf{W}}}R)

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{D_{t}}R)+K

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(T_{1-Y}(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{r}+T_{\bar{x}}\bar{R})T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}}R)

\displaystyle+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}(T_{g+{\underline{a}}}Y-\gamma R))+K

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{r}R)+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}Y)-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)+K,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{D_{t}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}W)=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(D_{t}\bar{r}W)+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{D_{t}}W)+K

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}((\gamma\bar{r}-T_{g+{\underline{a}}}(\bar{x}-T_{\bar{w}}\bar{Y}))W)-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R)+K

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}W)+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}W)-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)+K,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}{\mathbf{W}})=

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}Y)+K,

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}W)=

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}W)+K,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)+K,

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)-

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{x}W).

Finally, for the contributions of corrections of
\gamma^{3}
and
\gamma^{4}
terms, we get

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{D_{t}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}W)=

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(T_{D_{t}}\bar{u}W)-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{D_{t}}W)+K

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(T_{1-Y}(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{r}+T_{\bar{x}}\bar{R})W)+\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R)+K

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}W)+\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)+K,

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}W)=

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}W)+K,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)=

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)+K,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g^{2}}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}W)=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}W)+K,

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{3}}{2g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)-

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{r}W)-i\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g}{\mathbf{P}}(\bar{u}W).

Putting together all the contributions of the corrections, we get that the total contributions for the second equation is exactly
-\mathcal{K}_{0,0}+K
.
∎

5.
Energy estimates for the full system

In this section, we prove energy estimates for the water wave system (
1.7
), as in the statement of Theorem
1.3
.

As
({\mathbf{W}},R)
solve the linearized system (
4.1
), the results in the previous section show that the full system is well-posed for initial data in
\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{4}}
.
However, the estimates for the linearized system no longer hold for other Sobolev index
s\neq\frac{1}{4}
.
We consider in this section for general
s\geq 0
.

5.1.
Reduction to paradifferential linearized
({\mathbf{W}},R)
system

The system (
4.1
) follows from linearizing (
1.6
) and diagonalizing to switch to good variables.
In this section, we instead consider direct linearization from (
1.7
).
Let the linearized variables around a solution
({\mathbf{W}},R)
of the water waves system (
1.7
) be
(\hat{w},\hat{r})
.
We begin by computing the linearizations of the auxiliary functions:

\displaystyle\delta a=i[\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}(\bar{{\hat{r}}}R_{\alpha})+\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}(\bar{R}{\hat{r}}_{\alpha})-{\mathbf{P}}({\hat{r}}\bar{R}_{\alpha})-{\mathbf{P}}(R\bar{{\hat{r}}}_{\alpha})],

\displaystyle\delta{\underline{b}}=\delta b-i\frac{\gamma}{2}\delta b_{1},\quad\delta b=2\Re{\mathbf{P}}[(1-\bar{Y}){\hat{r}}-(1-\bar{Y})^{2}R\bar{{\hat{w}}}],

\displaystyle\delta b_{1}=2i\Im{\mathbf{P}}[(1-\bar{Y})\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}{\hat{w}}-(1-\bar{Y})^{2}W\bar{{\hat{w}}}],

\displaystyle\delta N=2\Re{\mathbf{P}}[\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}{\hat{w}}\bar{R}_{\alpha}+W\bar{{\hat{r}}}_{\alpha}-\bar{{\hat{w}}}R-\bar{W}_{\alpha}{\hat{r}}],

\displaystyle\delta M=2\Re{\mathbf{P}}[{\hat{r}}\bar{Y}_{\alpha}-2R(1-\bar{Y})\bar{{\hat{w}}}\bar{Y}_{\alpha}+R(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{{\hat{w}}}_{\alpha}-\bar{{\hat{r}}}_{\alpha}Y-\bar{R}_{\alpha}(1-Y)^{2}{\hat{w}}],

\displaystyle\delta M_{1}=2i\Im{\mathbf{P}}\partial_{\alpha}[\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}{\hat{w}}\bar{Y}+W(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{{\hat{w}}}],\quad\delta\underline{M}=\delta M-i\frac{\gamma}{2}\delta M_{1}.

Then the linearized system can be written as

\left\{\begin{aligned} D_{t}{\hat{w}}&+(1-\bar{Y})(1+W_{\alpha}){\hat{r}}_{\alpha}+(1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}{\hat{w}}=(1+W_{\alpha})\delta\underline{M}+\underline{M}{\hat{w}}-W_{\alpha\alpha}\delta{\underline{b}}\\
&+(1-\bar{Y})^{2}(1+W_{\alpha})R_{\alpha}\bar{{\hat{w}}}+i\gamma W_{\alpha}{\hat{w}}-i\frac{\gamma}{2}\bar{W}_{\alpha}{\hat{w}}-i\frac{\gamma}{2}W_{\alpha}\bar{{\hat{w}}},\\
D_{t}{\hat{r}}&+i\gamma{\hat{r}}-i(g+a)(1-Y)^{2}{\hat{w}}=-R_{\alpha}\delta{\underline{b}}-i(1-Y)\delta a+i\frac{\gamma}{2}(1-Y)^{2}{\hat{w}}(R+\bar{R})\\
&+i\frac{\gamma}{2}W_{\alpha}(1-Y)({\hat{r}}+\bar{{\hat{r}}})+i\frac{\gamma}{2}(1-Y)\delta N-i\frac{\gamma}{2}N(1-Y)^{2}{\hat{w}}.\end{aligned}\right.

Next, we rewrite the linearized system in the paradifferential framework.
We consider the cases where
{\hat{w}}
and
{\hat{r}}
terms are the highest frequencies for the paraproduct types contributions.
After applying the Littlewood-Paley projection
{\mathbf{P}}
, we can eliminate all
(\bar{{\hat{w}}},\bar{{\hat{r}}})
terms, as well as all
({\hat{w}},{\hat{r}})
terms inside the anti-holomorphic projection
\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}
.
Using (
1.8
) to simplify, we obtain the following linear paradifferential equations:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}T_{D_{t}}{\hat{w}}+T_{{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}}{\hat{w}}+\partial_{\alpha}T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{1+W_{\alpha}}{\hat{r}}-i\frac{\gamma}{2}\partial_{\alpha}T_{1+W_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}{\hat{w}}+i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\hat{w}}=0&\\
T_{D_{t}}{\hat{r}}+T_{{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}}{\hat{r}}+i\gamma{\hat{r}}-iT_{(1-Y)^{2}}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}{\hat{w}}+T_{\underline{M}}{\hat{r}}+i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{b_{\alpha}+M}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}{\hat{w}}+\gamma T_{\Im W_{\alpha}Y}{\hat{r}}=0.&\end{array}\right.

We claim that the differentiated system (
1.7
) can be recast in the above paradifferential type equations with source terms.
We let
(G,K)
be the favorable balanced cubic or higher source terms that satisfy

(5.1)

\|(G,K)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{s}}\lesssim_{\underline{A}}\underline{A}^{2}_{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\|({\mathbf{W}},R)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{s}}+\gamma\|({\mathbf{W}},R)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}\right).

The rest of the source terms are either quadratic in
({\mathbf{W}},R)
or unbalanced cubic terms.
These unfavorable source terms will be later removed by paradifferential normal form corrections.
This reduction result is stated as following:

Lemma 5.1
.

The differentiated system (
1.7
) can be rewritten as paradifferential equations for the variable
({\hat{w}},{\hat{r}})=({\mathbf{W}},R)
of the form

(5.2)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}T_{D_{t}}{\hat{w}}+T_{{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}}{\hat{w}}+\partial_{\alpha}T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{1+W_{\alpha}}{\hat{r}}-i\frac{\gamma}{2}\partial_{\alpha}T_{1+W_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}{\hat{w}}+i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\hat{w}}=\mathcal{G}({\mathbf{W}},R)+G&\\
T_{D_{t}}{\hat{r}}+i\gamma{\hat{r}}+T_{{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}}{\hat{r}}-iT_{(1-Y)^{2}}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}{\hat{w}}-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{b_{\alpha}}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}{\hat{w}}=\mathcal{K}({\mathbf{W}},R)+K,&\end{array}\right.

where
(G,K)
are balanced cubic source terms that satisfy (
5.1
), and non-perturbative source terms are given by

\left\{\begin{aligned} \mathcal{G}({\mathbf{W}},R)=&-\partial_{\alpha}[\Pi({\mathbf{W}},T_{1-\bar{Y}}R)+\Pi({\mathbf{W}},T_{1-Y}\bar{R})-\Pi(\bar{Y},T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)]\\
&+i\frac{\gamma}{2}\partial_{\alpha}[\Pi({\mathbf{W}},T_{1-\bar{Y}}W)-\Pi({\mathbf{W}},T_{1-Y}\bar{W})-\Pi(X,\bar{Y})],\\
\mathcal{K}({\mathbf{W}},R)=&-T_{1-\bar{Y}}\Pi(R_{\alpha},R)-T_{1-Y}\Pi(R_{\alpha},\bar{R})-T_{1-Y}\Pi(\bar{R}_{\alpha},R)\\
&+i\frac{\gamma}{2}\Pi(R_{\alpha},T_{1-\bar{Y}}W-T_{1-Y}\bar{W})-iT_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\Pi(Y,{\mathbf{W}})\\
&+i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{1-Y}\Pi(W,\bar{R}_{\alpha})-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{1-Y}\Pi(\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},R)+i\frac{\gamma}{2}\Pi(R+\bar{R},Y).\end{aligned}\right.

Again we include the Littlewood-Paley projection
{\mathbf{P}}
in
\Pi
implicitly as in the previous section.

Proof.

Recall that the differentiated system (
1.7
) can be rewritten as

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}D_{t}{\mathbf{W}}+(1+\mathbf{W})(1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}=(1+\mathbf{W})\underline{M}+i\dfrac{\gamma}{2}\mathbf{W}(\mathbf{W}-\bar{\mathbf{W}})&\\
D_{t}R+i\gamma R=i[g-(g+{\underline{a}})(1-Y)]+i\frac{\gamma}{2}(R+\bar{R}).&\end{array}\right.

Here the system is written in an algebraic way for convenience.
We apply the Littlewood-Paley projection
{\mathbf{P}}
to above system to eliminate the anti-holomorphic parts.
For our computation below, the balanced cubic terms are put in
(G,K)
without further specification.

(1)

For the first term in the first equation, we expand
{\underline{b}}
to write

\displaystyle{\mathbf{P}}D_{t}{\mathbf{W}}

\displaystyle=T_{D_{t}}{\mathbf{W}}+T_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}{\mathbf{P}}{\underline{b}}+\Pi({\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha},{\underline{b}})

\displaystyle=T_{D_{t}}{\mathbf{W}}+T_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}W

\displaystyle+\Pi({\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha},T_{1-\bar{Y}}R+T_{1-Y}\bar{R})-i\frac{\gamma}{2}\Pi({\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha},T_{1-\bar{Y}}W-T_{1-Y}\bar{W})+G.

For the second term in the first equation, we expand using paraproducts,

\displaystyle{\mathbf{P}}[(1+{\mathbf{W}})(1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}]

\displaystyle=T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{R_{\alpha}}{\mathbf{W}}

\displaystyle-T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}\Pi(\bar{Y},R_{\alpha})+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\Pi({\mathbf{W}},R_{\alpha})+G.

For the first source term in the first equation, we use (
1.8
) and (
3.7
) to write

\displaystyle(1+{\mathbf{W}})\underline{M}

\displaystyle=T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}\underline{M}+G=T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}{\mathbf{P}}\left[R\bar{Y}_{\alpha}-\bar{R}_{\alpha}Y-i\frac{\gamma}{2}(W\bar{Y})_{\alpha}\right]+G

\displaystyle=T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}\left(T_{\bar{Y}_{\alpha}}R-T_{\bar{R}_{\alpha}}Y-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{\bar{Y}_{\alpha}}W-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{W}}\right)

\displaystyle+T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}\left[\Pi(\bar{Y}_{\alpha},R)-\Pi(\bar{R}_{\alpha},Y)-i\frac{\gamma}{2}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(W,\bar{Y})\right]+G.

For the last term in the first equation,

i\dfrac{\gamma}{2}\mathbf{W}(\mathbf{W}-\bar{\mathbf{W}})=iT_{\frac{\gamma}{2}({\mathbf{W}}-\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})}{\mathbf{W}}+i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{W}}+i\frac{\gamma}{2}\Pi({\mathbf{W}},{\mathbf{W}}-\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}).

Combining all these terms, we use (
3.11
) to get

\displaystyle T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{R_{\alpha}}{\mathbf{W}}+T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{\bar{R}_{\alpha}}Y-iT_{\frac{\gamma}{2}({\mathbf{W}}-\bar{{\mathbf{W}}})}{\mathbf{W}}=T_{{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}}+G,

\displaystyle T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}-T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{\bar{Y}_{\alpha}}R+T_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R=\partial_{\alpha}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma}{2}(-T_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}W+T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}T_{\bar{Y}_{\alpha}}W+T_{\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{W}}-T_{{\mathbf{W}}}{\mathbf{W}})=-i\frac{\gamma}{2}\partial_{\alpha}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}W+i\frac{\gamma}{2}W.

Hence, we obtain the paradifferential
{\mathbf{W}}
equation.

(2)

For the second
R
equation, we expand in the similar way to write

\displaystyle{\mathbf{P}}D_{t}R

\displaystyle=T_{D_{t}}R+T_{R_{\alpha}}{\mathbf{P}}{\underline{b}}+\Pi(R_{\alpha},{\underline{b}})

\displaystyle=T_{D_{t}}R+T_{R_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{R_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}W

\displaystyle+\Pi(R_{\alpha},T_{1-\bar{Y}}R+T_{1-Y}\bar{R})-i\frac{\gamma}{2}\Pi(R_{\alpha},T_{1-\bar{Y}}W-T_{1-Y}\bar{W})+K.

For the first term on the right-hand side of the second equation,

\displaystyle i{\mathbf{P}}[g-(g+{\underline{a}})(1-Y)]

\displaystyle=iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}Y-iT_{1-Y}{\mathbf{P}}{\underline{a}}+i\Pi({\underline{a}},Y)

\displaystyle=iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}Y-T_{1-Y}T_{\bar{R}_{\alpha}}R-T_{1-Y}\Pi(\bar{R}_{\alpha},R)

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{1-Y}R+i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{1-Y}T_{\bar{R}_{\alpha}}W-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{1-Y}T_{\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}}R

\displaystyle+i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{1-Y}\Pi(W,\bar{R}_{\alpha})-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{1-Y}\Pi(\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},R)+i\frac{\gamma}{2}\Pi(R+\bar{R},Y)+K,

where again using
(4)
of Lemma
3.9
, we get

iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}Y=iT_{(1-Y)^{2}}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}{\mathbf{W}}-iT_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\Pi(Y,{\mathbf{W}})+K.

Similar to the case of the first equation, we combine using (
1.8
) and (
3.7
),

\displaystyle-T_{R_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R-T_{1-Y}T_{\bar{R}_{\alpha}}R-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{1-Y}R-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{1-Y}T_{\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}}R+i\frac{\gamma}{2}R

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{R_{\alpha}(1-\bar{Y})+\bar{R}_{\alpha}(1-Y)}R-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{1-Y}R+i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{1-Y}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{1-Y}T_{\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}}R+K

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{R_{\alpha}(1-\bar{Y})+\bar{R}_{\alpha}(1-Y)}R+i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{{\mathbf{W}}-\bar{{\mathbf{W}}}}R+K=-T_{{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}}R+K,

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{R_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}W-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{1-Y}T_{\bar{R}_{\alpha}}W=-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{b_{\alpha}}W+K.

Hence, we obtain the paradifferential
R
equation.

∎

The rest of Section
5
is devoted into the study of paradifferential system (
5.2
).

5.2.
Well-posedness for the paradifferential flow

In this subsection, we first consider the linear part of the paradifferential equations (
5.2
), namely

(5.3)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}T_{D_{t}}{\hat{w}}+T_{{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}}{\hat{w}}+\partial_{\alpha}T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{1+W_{\alpha}}{\hat{r}}-i\frac{\gamma}{2}\partial_{\alpha}T_{1+W_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}{\hat{w}}+i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\hat{w}}=0&\\
T_{D_{t}}{\hat{r}}+i\gamma{\hat{r}}+T_{{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}}{\hat{r}}-iT_{(1-Y)^{2}}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}{\hat{w}}-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{b_{\alpha}}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}{\hat{w}}=0.&\end{array}\right.

Although it is possible to perform the analysis similar as previouly done in Section
4
, a more convenient way is to reduce (
5.3
) to the paradifferential equations (
4.5
).
The result is as follows:

Proposition 5.2
.

Suppose that
({\hat{w}},{\hat{r}})
solve (
5.3
), then there exists a bounded linear transformation which is independent of the Sobolev index
s
, such that it turns
({\hat{w}},{\hat{r}})
into
(w,r)
that solve (
4.5
).
In addition, for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

(1)

Invertibility:

\|(w_{\alpha},r_{\alpha})-({\hat{w}},{\hat{r}})\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{s}}\lesssim_{\underline{A}}{\underline{A}}\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{s}}.

(2)

Perturbative source term:

\|(G,K)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{s}}\lesssim_{A}\underline{A}^{2}_{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{s}}+\gamma\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}\right).

As a consequence, Proposition
4.3
holds with (
5.3
) in place of (
4.3
).

Proof.

When computing the linearized system, the linearization of
R
is given by

\delta R=\frac{r_{\alpha}+R_{\alpha}w}{1+{\mathbf{W}}}.

This suggests that the connection between
(w,r)
and
({\hat{w}},{\hat{r}})
is given by the relation

({\hat{w}},{\hat{r}})=\left(w_{\alpha},\frac{r_{\alpha}+R_{\alpha}w}{1+{\mathbf{W}}}\right).

Given a solution
({\hat{w}},{\hat{r}})
to (
5.3
), we therefore define
(w,r)

at the paradifferential level:

(5.4)

(w,r):=(\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}{\hat{w}},\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}{\hat{r}}-\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}T_{R_{\alpha}}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}{\hat{w}}).

Clearly, this definition of
(w,r)
satisfies the invertibility property.
It remains to show that
(w,r)
defined in (
5.4
) satisfy the paradifferential system (
4.5
) with perturbative source terms.

We plug in the relation (
5.4
) into (
4.3
) and compute the corresponding source terms.
Here, the source terms are acceptable if they satisfy

\|G\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}+\|K\|_{\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim_{\underline{A}}{\underline{A}}^{2}_{\frac{1}{4}}\|(w,r)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{s+1}}.

For the first equation,

\displaystyle\partial_{\alpha}(T_{D_{t}}w+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r+T_{(1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}}w+\gamma T_{\Im{\mathbf{W}}}w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{D_{t}}{\hat{w}}+T_{{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}}{\hat{w}}+\partial_{\alpha}T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{1+W_{\alpha}}{\hat{r}}+\gamma\partial_{\alpha}T_{\Im W_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}{\hat{w}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{D_{t}}{\hat{w}}+T_{{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}}{\hat{w}}+\partial_{\alpha}T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{1+W_{\alpha}}{\hat{r}}-i\frac{\gamma}{2}\partial_{\alpha}T_{1+W_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial^{-1}{\hat{w}}+i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\hat{w}}

\displaystyle+\left(\gamma\partial_{\alpha}T_{\Im W_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}{\hat{w}}+i\frac{\gamma}{2}\partial_{\alpha}T_{1+W_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial^{-1}{\hat{w}}-i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\hat{w}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(T_{D_{t}}{\hat{w}}+T_{{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}}{\hat{w}}+\partial_{\alpha}T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{1+W_{\alpha}}{\hat{r}}-i\frac{\gamma}{2}\partial_{\alpha}T_{1+W_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial^{-1}{\hat{w}}+i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\hat{w}}\right)+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle G.

For the second equation, we use Lemma
2.5
to distribute para-material derivatives, and use para-commutators (
2.11
), para-products (
2.12
) to write

\displaystyle\partial_{\alpha}(T_{D_{t}}r+i\gamma r-iT_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{D_{t}}(T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}{\hat{r}}-T_{R_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}{\hat{w}})+T_{{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}}(T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}{\hat{r}}-T_{R_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}{\hat{w}})

\displaystyle+i\gamma(T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}{\hat{r}}-T_{R_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}{\hat{w}})-i\partial_{\alpha}T_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}w

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{D_{t}{\mathbf{W}}}{\hat{r}}+T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{D_{t}}{\hat{r}}-T_{R_{\alpha}}T_{D_{t}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}{\hat{w}}-T_{\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}R}w+T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}}{\hat{r}}

\displaystyle+i\gamma T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}{\hat{r}}-i\gamma T_{R_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}{\hat{w}}+iT_{Y_{\alpha}}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}w-iT_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}w_{\alpha}-iT_{1-Y}T_{{\underline{a}}_{\alpha}}w+K

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}(T_{D_{t}}+T_{{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}}){\hat{r}}-T_{R_{\alpha}}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}(T_{D_{t}}+T_{{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}}){\hat{w}}+T_{D_{t}{\mathbf{W}}}{\hat{r}}-T_{\partial_{\alpha}D_{t}R}w

\displaystyle+i\gamma T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}{\hat{r}}-i\gamma T_{R_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}{\hat{w}}+iT_{Y_{\alpha}}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}w-iT_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}w_{\alpha}-iT_{1-Y}T_{{\underline{a}}_{\alpha}}w+K

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle iT_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{(g+{\underline{a}})(1-Y)^{2}}{\hat{w}}-i\gamma T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}{\hat{r}}+i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{b_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}{\hat{w}}+T_{R_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}{\hat{r}}

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{R_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}{\hat{w}}+i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{R_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}{\hat{w}}-T_{(1+{\mathbf{W}})(1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}}{\hat{r}}+i\gamma T_{R_{\alpha}}w+iT_{\partial_{\alpha}(g+{\underline{a}})(1-Y)}w

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{R_{\alpha}+\bar{R}_{\alpha}}w+i\gamma T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}{\hat{r}}-i\gamma T_{R_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}{\hat{w}}-iT_{\partial_{\alpha}(g+{\underline{a}})(1-Y)}w-iT_{1-Y}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}w_{\alpha}+K

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{b_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}{\hat{w}}-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{R_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}{\hat{w}}-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{\bar{R}_{\alpha}}w+K

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K.

Here we have harmlessly replaced
T_{D_{t}}{\mathbf{W}}
and
T_{D_{t}}R
by
D_{t}{\mathbf{W}}
and
D_{t}R
.

Finally, the
\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{s+1}
well-posedness of (
4.3
) proved in Proposition
4.3
implies the
\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{s}
well-posedness of
(
5.3
).
∎

5.3.
The paradifferential normal form transformation

Having settled the local well-posedness of the linear system (
5.3
), we now consider the right-hand side source terms of (
5.2
).
Although the source terms
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{K})
are not directly perturbative, we are able to use a paradifferential normal form transformation to eliminate them modulo perturbative terms.
Precisely, we can construct paradifferential normal form variables
({\mathbf{W}}_{NF},R_{NF})
that satisfy the following result:

Proposition 5.3
.

Suppose that
({\mathbf{W}},R)
solve the system (
1.7
), then there exist paradifferential normal form variables
({\mathbf{W}}_{NF},R_{NF})
that satisfy the following system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}T_{D_{t}}{\mathbf{W}}_{NF}+T_{{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}}{\mathbf{W}}_{NF}+\partial_{\alpha}T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{1+W_{\alpha}}R_{NF}-i\frac{\gamma}{2}\partial_{\alpha}T_{1+W_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}{\mathbf{W}}_{NF}+i\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbf{W}}_{NF}=\tilde{\mathcal{G}}&\\
T_{D_{t}}R_{NF}+i\gamma R_{NF}+T_{{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}}R_{NF}-iT_{(1-Y)^{2}}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}{\mathbf{W}}_{NF}-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{b_{\alpha}}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}{\mathbf{W}}_{NF}=\tilde{\mathcal{K}},&\end{array}\right.

such that for any
s\geq 0
, we have

(1)

Invertibility:

\|({\mathbf{W}}_{NF},R_{NF})-({\mathbf{W}},R)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{s}}\lesssim_{\underline{A}}{\underline{A}}\left(\|({\mathbf{W}},R)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{s}}+\gamma^{2}\|({\mathbf{W}},R)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{s-1}}\right).

(2)

Perturbative source terms:

\|(\tilde{\mathcal{G}},\tilde{\mathcal{K}})\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{s}}\lesssim_{\underline{A}}{\underline{A}}^{2}_{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\|({\mathbf{W}},R)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{s}}+\gamma^{2}\|({\mathbf{W}},R)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{s-1}}\right).

Proof.

We choose the paradifferential normal form variables
({\mathbf{W}}_{NF},R_{NF})
to be
({\mathbf{W}}_{NF},R_{NF})

:=({\mathbf{W}}+\tilde{{\mathbf{W}}},R+\tilde{R})
, where the paradifferential corrections
(\tilde{{\mathbf{W}}},\tilde{R})
are given by

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathbf{W}}}=

\displaystyle-\partial_{\alpha}\Pi({\mathbf{W}},2\Re X)-\frac{\gamma}{2g}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi({\mathbf{W}},2\Re Z)-\frac{\gamma}{2g}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(R,2\Re X)-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi({\mathbf{W}},2\Im U)

\displaystyle+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,X)+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,2\Re X)-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(R,2\Re Z)+i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,2\Re Z)

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(R,2\Im U)+i\frac{\gamma^{4}}{2g^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,2\Im U),

\displaystyle\tilde{R}=

\displaystyle-\Pi(R_{\alpha},2\Re X)-\Pi(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},R)-\frac{\gamma}{2g}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(R,2\Re Z)+i\frac{\gamma}{2}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,2\Re X)

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma}{4}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,X)-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(R,2\Im U)+i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,2\Re Z)+i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,2\Im U).

We remark that the quadratic part of this paradifferential correction is nothing but roughly the balanced part of the derivative of the normal form transformation (
1.10
), after switching to the good variables
({\mathbf{W}},R)
.

Clearly,
(\tilde{{\mathbf{W}}},\tilde{R})
satisfy the invertibility property using direct computation, it only suffices to check that the source terms
(\tilde{\mathcal{G}},\tilde{\mathcal{K}})
are perturbative.

We insert these corrections into the system (
5.3
) and compute the corresponding source terms.
For both equations, the terms having
T_{{\underline{b}}_{\alpha}}
are perturbative.
For convenience of the computation below, we recall in Section
3.2
, the leading terms of para-material derivatives.

\displaystyle T_{D_{t}}{\mathbf{W}}+T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}=E_{1},\quad

\displaystyle\|E_{1}\|_{BMO}\lesssim_{\underline{A}}{\underline{A}}^{2}_{\frac{1}{4}},

\displaystyle T_{D_{t}}R-iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}Y+i\gamma R=E_{2},\quad

\displaystyle\|E_{2}\|_{BMO^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim_{\underline{A}}{\underline{A}}^{2}_{\frac{1}{4}},

\displaystyle T_{D_{t}}X+T_{1-\bar{Y}}R=E_{3},\quad

\displaystyle\||D|E_{3}\|_{BMO}+\gamma^{2}\|E_{3}\|_{BMO}\lesssim_{{\underline{A}^{\sharp}}}\underline{A}^{2}_{\frac{1}{4}},

\displaystyle T_{D_{t}}Z-igX+i\gamma Z=E_{4},\quad

\displaystyle\gamma^{2}\||D|^{\frac{1}{2}}E_{4}\|_{BMO}+\gamma^{3}\|E_{4}\|_{BMO}\lesssim_{{\underline{A}^{\sharp}}}\underline{A}^{2}_{\frac{1}{4}},

\displaystyle T_{D_{t}}U+T_{1-\bar{Y}}Z=E_{5},\quad

\displaystyle\gamma^{2}\||D|E_{5}\|_{BMO}\lesssim_{{\underline{A}^{\sharp}}}\underline{A}^{2}_{\frac{1}{4}}.

We will use Lemma
2.5
to distribute para-material derivatives.
Let
(G,K)
be the good source terms that satisfy the perturbative source terms bound in the proposition.
In the following, we will put the perturbative source terms into
(G,K)
for simplicity.

We first compute the source term of the first equation of (
5.3
).
For the first term of the first equation,

\displaystyle-T_{D_{t}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi({\mathbf{W}},2\Re X)

\displaystyle=\partial_{\alpha}\left(\Pi(T_{(1+{\mathbf{W}})(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha},2\Re X)+\Pi({\mathbf{W}},T_{1-\bar{Y}}R+T_{1-Y}\bar{R})\right)+G,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{D_{t}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi({\mathbf{W}},2\Re Z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma}{2g}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\Pi(T_{(1+{\mathbf{W}})(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha},2\Re Z)+\Pi({\mathbf{W}},2g\Im X-2\gamma\Im Z)\right)+G,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{D_{t}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(R,2\Re X)

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma}{2g}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\Pi(-iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}Y+i\gamma R,2\Re X)+\Pi(R,T_{1-\bar{Y}}R+T_{1-Y}\bar{R})\right)+G,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{D_{t}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi({\mathbf{W}},2\Im U)

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\Pi(T_{(1+{\mathbf{W}})(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha},2\Im U)+\Pi({\mathbf{W}},2\Im T_{1-\bar{Y}}Z)\right)+G,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{D_{t}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,X)

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(T_{1-\bar{Y}}R,X)+G,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{D_{t}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,2\Re X)

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\Pi(T_{1-\bar{Y}}R,2\Re X)+\Pi(X,T_{1-\bar{Y}}R+T_{1-Y}\bar{R})\right)+G,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}T_{D_{t}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(R,2\Re Z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\Pi(-iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}Y+i\gamma R,2\Re Z)+\Pi(R,2g\Im X-2\gamma\Im Z)\right)+G,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{D_{t}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,2\Re Z)

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\Pi(T_{1-\bar{Y}}R,2\Re Z)+\Pi(X,2g\Im X-2\gamma\Im Z)\right)+G,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{D_{t}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(R,2\Im U)

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\Pi(-iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}Y+i\gamma R,2\Im U)+\Pi(R,2\Im T_{1-\bar{Y}}Z)\right)+G,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{4}}{2g^{2}}T_{D_{t}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,2\Im U)

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma^{4}}{2g^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\Pi(T_{1-\bar{Y}}R,2\Im U)+\Pi(X,2\Im T_{1-\bar{Y}}Z)\right)+G.

For the third term of the first equation, we apply the para-associativity (
2.14
) and the relations (
3.11
), (
3.12
), (
3.13
),

\displaystyle-\partial_{\alpha}T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}\Pi(R_{\alpha},2\Re X)

\displaystyle=-\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(T_{(1-\bar{Y})(1+{\mathbf{W}})}R_{\alpha},2\Re X)+G,

\displaystyle-\partial_{\alpha}T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}\Pi(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},R)

\displaystyle=-\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(\bar{Y},T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}R)+G,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}\partial_{\alpha}T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(R,2\Re Z)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma}{2g}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\Pi(T_{(1-\bar{Y})(1+{\mathbf{W}})}R_{\alpha},2\Re Z)+\Pi(R,2\Re T_{1-\bar{Y}}R)\right)+G,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma}{2}\partial_{\alpha}T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,2\Re X)

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma}{2}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\Pi(Y,2\Re X)+\Pi(X,2\Re{\mathbf{W}})\right)+G,

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma}{4}\partial_{\alpha}T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,X)

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma}{2}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi({\mathbf{W}},T_{1-\bar{Y}}W)+G,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}\partial_{\alpha}T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(R,2\Im U)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\Pi(T_{(1+{\mathbf{W}})(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha},2\Im U)+\Pi(T_{1-\bar{Y}}R,2\Im X)\right)+G,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}\partial_{\alpha}T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,2\Re Z)

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\Pi({\mathbf{W}},2\Re Z)+\Pi(X,T_{1-\bar{Y}}R+T_{1-Y}\bar{R})\right)+G,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}\partial_{\alpha}T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,2\Im U)

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\Pi({\mathbf{W}},2\Im U)+\Pi(X,2\Im X)\right)+G.

Here, when the derivatives fall on the paradifferential coefficients
T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}
or
T_{1-\bar{Y}}
, these terms are perturbative and may go to
G
.

For the last two terms of the first equation, they are

-i\frac{\gamma}{2}\partial_{\alpha}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}\tilde{{\mathbf{W}}}+i\frac{\gamma}{2}\tilde{{\mathbf{W}}}=-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{{\mathbf{W}}-\bar{Y}-{\mathbf{W}}\bar{Y}}\tilde{{\mathbf{W}}}-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\tilde{{\mathbf{W}}}+i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{1+{\mathbf{W}}}T_{\bar{Y}_{\alpha}}\tilde{{\mathbf{W}}}.

Since the paradifferential correction
\tilde{{\mathbf{W}}}
satisfies

\|\tilde{{\mathbf{W}}}\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{s}}\lesssim_{\underline{A}}{\underline{A}}\left(\|({\mathbf{W}},R)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{s}}+\gamma^{2}\|({\mathbf{W}},R)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{s-1}}\right),

the sum of the last two terms can be absorbed into
G
.

Gathering all terms for the first equation, we see that the contribution of paradifferential corrections cancel the source term
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}
modulo perturbative terms.

Next, we perform the computation for the contribution of the paradifferential corrections in the second equation of (
5.3
).
For the first term of the second equation,

\displaystyle-T_{D_{t}}\Pi(R_{\alpha},2\Re X)

\displaystyle=-i\Pi(T_{g+{\underline{a}}}Y_{\alpha}-\gamma R_{\alpha},2\Re X)+\Pi(R_{\alpha},T_{1-\bar{Y}}R+T_{1-Y}\bar{R})+K,

\displaystyle-T_{D_{t}}\Pi(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},R)

\displaystyle=T_{1-Y}\Pi(\bar{R}_{\alpha},R)-i\Pi(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},T_{g+{\underline{a}}}Y-\gamma R)+K,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{D_{t}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(R,2\Re Z)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma}{2g}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\Pi(iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}Y-i\gamma R,2\Re Z)+\Pi(R,-2g\Im X+2\gamma\Im Z)\right)+K,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{D_{t}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,\bar{X})

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma}{2}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\Pi(T_{1-\bar{Y}}R,\bar{X})+\Pi(T_{1-Y}\bar{R},X)\right)+K,

\displaystyle-i\frac{\gamma}{4}T_{D_{t}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,X)

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma}{2}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(T_{1-\bar{Y}}R,X)+K,

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{D_{t}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(R,2\Im U)

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\Pi(i\gamma R-iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}Y,2\Im U)+\Pi(R,2\Im T_{1-\bar{Y}}Z)\right)+K,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{D_{t}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,2\Re Z)

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\Pi(T_{1-\bar{Y}}R,2\Re Z)+\Pi(X,2g\Im X-2\gamma\Im Z)\right)+K,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}T_{D_{t}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,2\Im U)

\displaystyle=-i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\Pi(T_{1-\bar{Y}}R,2\Im U)+\Pi(X,2\Im T_{1-\bar{Y}}Z)\right)+K.

The second term of the second equation is given by

\displaystyle-i\gamma\Pi(R_{\alpha},2\Re X)-i\gamma\Pi(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{{\mathbf{W}}},R)-i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(R,2\Re Z)-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,2\Re X)

\displaystyle+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,X)-i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{2g}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(R,2\Im U)-\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,2\Re Z)-\frac{\gamma^{4}}{4g}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,2\Im U).

For the fourth term of the second equation, the contribution of
T_{\underline{a}}
is perturbative, and may be absorbed into
K
.
We apply the para-associativity (
2.14
) to write,

\displaystyle iT_{(1-Y)^{2}}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi({\mathbf{W}},2\Re X)

\displaystyle=i\Pi(T_{g+{\underline{a}}}Y_{\alpha},2\Re X)

\displaystyle+iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}\left(T_{1-Y}\Pi({\mathbf{W}},Y)+\Pi(Y,\bar{X}_{\alpha})\right)+K,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{(1-Y)^{2}}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi({\mathbf{W}},2\Re Z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma}{2g}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}Y,2\Re Z)+K,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma}{2g}T_{(1-Y)^{2}}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(R,2\Re X)

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma}{2}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(T_{1-\bar{Y}}R,2\Re X)+K,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}T_{(1-Y)^{2}}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi({\mathbf{W}},2\Im U)

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma^{2}}{2g}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(iT_{g+{\underline{a}}}Y,2\Im U)+K,

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{(1-Y)^{2}}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,X)

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,X)+K,

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}T_{(1-Y)^{2}}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,2\Re X)

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}\partial_{\alpha}(X,2\Re X)+K,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g^{2}}T_{(1-Y)^{2}}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(R,2\Re Z)

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4g}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(R,2\Re Z)+K,

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{(1-Y)^{2}}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,2\Re Z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,2\Re Z)+K,

\displaystyle i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g^{2}}T_{(1-Y)^{2}}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(R,2\Im U)

\displaystyle=i\frac{\gamma^{3}}{4g}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(R,2\Im U)+K,

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{4}}{2g^{2}}T_{(1-Y)^{2}}T_{g+{\underline{a}}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,2\Im U)

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma^{4}}{2g}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(X,2\Im U)+K.

The last term of the left-hand side of the second equation
-i\frac{\gamma}{2}T_{b_{\alpha}}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}\tilde{{\mathbf{W}}}
is perturbative and may absorbed into
K
.
After cancellations, the source term of the paradifferential corrections of the second equation equals
-\tilde{\mathcal{K}}
modulo perturbative
K
terms.
Therefore, we construct the paradifferential normal form variables
({\mathbf{W}}_{NF},R_{NF})
that satisfy the desired properties.
∎

Having switched into the paradifferential normal form variables
({\mathbf{W}}_{NF},R_{NF})
, the differentiated system (
1.7
) is reduced to the paradifferential equations (
5.3
) with perturbative source terms.
Theorem
1.3
then follows directly from the last part of Proposition
5.2
.

6.
The proof of local well-posedness

In this section, we prove the main result of this article, namely the low regularity well-posedness Theorem
1.4
.
The result from
[
17
]
asserts that the local well-posedness holds for more regular data.
We will first establish the
\mathcal{H}^{n}
bounds for regular solutions.
Then, we use those regular solutions as starting points to construct rough solutions.
Precisely, we obtain regular solutions by truncating the rough initial data in frequency, so that we get a continuous family of solutions, thereby estimating only a solution for linearized equations at each step.
Finally, we prove the continuous dependence on the initial data in
\mathcal{H}^{\frac{3}{4}}
.

Note that we can make use of the space-time scaling (
1.3
).
By choosing the scaling parameter
\lambda
small enough, we can make
{\underline{A}^{\sharp}}\ll 1
, at the price to turn the vorticity
\gamma
into
\lambda\gamma
.
The argument for the proof below is similar to the proof in
[
4
]
.
For simplicity, we only give an outline of the approach here.
Please check Section 7 of
[
4
]
for detailed proof such as the use of
frequency envelopes
.

Outline of proof of Theorem
1.4
.

The proof of the theorem is divided into the following four steps:

(1)

\mathcal{H}^{s}
bounds for regular solutions.

For simplicity, here we only outline the case for integer
n\geq 1
.
Suppose we have an
\mathcal{H}^{n}
solution
({\mathbf{W}},R)
that satisfies the initial condition

\|({\mathbf{W}}_{0},R_{0})\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{3}{4}}}+\gamma^{2}\|({\mathbf{W}}_{0},R_{0})\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{-\frac{1}{4}}}\leq\mathcal{M}_{0}\ll 1.

In order to show that there exists a time
T=T(\mathcal{M}_{0})
such that the solution exists in
C([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{n})
and satisfies the bounds

(6.1)

\displaystyle\|({\mathbf{W}},R)\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{3}{4}})}+\gamma^{2}\|({\mathbf{W}},R)\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{-\frac{1}{4}})}<\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{M}_{0}),

(6.2)

\displaystyle\|({\mathbf{W}},R)\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{n})}\leq C(\mathcal{M}_{0})\|({\mathbf{W}}_{0},R_{0})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{n}},

we make the bootstrap assumption

\|({\mathbf{W}},R)\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{3}{4}})}+\gamma^{2}\|({\mathbf{W}},R)\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{-\frac{1}{4}})}<2\mathcal{M}.

Using the bootstrap assumption and Sobolev embedding, we are able to bound the control parameters

{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\lesssim\mathcal{M},\quad{\underline{A}^{\sharp}_{\frac{1}{4}}}\lesssim\mathcal{M}.

Applying the energy estimate with
s=\frac{3}{4}
in Theorem
1.3
, we have

\|({\mathbf{W}},R)(t)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{3}{4}}}+\gamma^{2}\|({\mathbf{W}},R)(t)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{-\frac{1}{4}}}\lesssim e^{Ct}\left(\|({\mathbf{W}}_{0},R_{0})\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{3}{4}}}+\gamma^{2}\|({\mathbf{W}}_{0},R_{0})\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{-\frac{1}{4}}}\right)\lesssim e^{Ct}\mathcal{M}_{0}.

By choosing large
\mathcal{M}
and suitable
T
, the bound (
6.1
) holds for
t\in[0,T]
.

Applying Theorem
1.3
and Gronwall’s inequality for each integer
k
between
1
and
n
, we have

\|({\mathbf{W}},R)(t)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{k}}+\gamma^{2}\|({\mathbf{W}},R)(t)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{k-1}}\lesssim e^{Ct}\left(\|({\mathbf{W}}_{0},R_{0})\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{k}}+\gamma^{2}\|({\mathbf{W}}_{0},R_{0})\|_{\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{k-1}}\right).

Summing above inequalities for
k
from
1
to
n
, we get (
6.2
).
Therefore we have the
\mathcal{H}^{n}
bounds for regular solutions up to time
T
.

(2)
Construction of rough solutions,
({\mathbf{W}},R)\in\mathcal{H}^{\frac{3}{4}}
.

We regularize the initial data,
(W_{<k}(0),Q_{<k}(0))
and
({\mathbf{W}}_{<k}(0),R_{<k}(0))
by truncating at frequency
2^{k}
.
We are able to establish the bound of regularized initial data using frequency envelopes.
The corresponding solutions will be regular, with a uniform lifespan bound.
Here
k
can be viewed as a continuous parameter rather than a
discrete parameter.
Then

(w^{k},r^{k})=(\partial_{k}W_{<k},\partial_{k}Q_{<k}-R_{<k}\partial_{k}W_{<k})

solve the corresponding linearized equations around
({\mathbf{W}}_{<k},R_{<k})
.
For the high-frequency part of the regularized solutions, we apply the energy estimates for the full equation Theorem
1.3
.
Next, using Theorem
4.2
for the linearized variables
(w^{k},r^{k})
, one can establish the difference bound
({\mathbf{W}}_{<k+1}-{\mathbf{W}}_{<k},R_{<k+1}-R_{<k})
in
\mathcal{H}^{\frac{3}{4}}
.
Summing up with respect to
k
, it follows that the sequence
({\mathbf{W}}_{<k},R_{<k})
converges to a solution
({\mathbf{W}},R)
with uniform
\mathcal{H}^{\frac{3}{4}}
bound in time interval
[0,T]
.

(3)
Continuous dependence on the data for rough solutions.

Consider an arbitrary sequence
({\mathbf{W}}_{j},R_{j})(0)
that converges to
({\mathbf{W}}_{0},R_{0})
in
\mathcal{H}^{\frac{3}{4}}
topology.
Using again the frequency truncation, we get the approximate solutions
({\mathbf{W}}_{j}^{k},R_{j}^{k})
, respectively
({\mathbf{W}}^{k},R^{k})
.
Due to the continuous dependence for the regular solutions which is proved in Theorem
1.1
of
[
15
]
, we have for each
k

({\mathbf{W}}^{k}_{j},R^{k}_{j})-({\mathbf{W}}^{k},R^{k})\rightarrow 0\quad\text{in }\mathcal{H}^{n},\quad n\geq 1.

On the other hand, letting
k
go to infinity, we have for the initial data

({\mathbf{W}}^{k}_{j},R^{k}_{j})(0)-({\mathbf{W}}_{j},R_{j})(0)\rightarrow 0\quad\text{in }\mathcal{H}^{\frac{3}{4}},\quad\text{uniformly in }j.

Using the frequency envelope analysis, we further get the uniform convergence for the solution:

({\mathbf{W}}^{k}_{j},R^{k}_{j})-({\mathbf{W}}_{j},R_{j})\rightarrow 0\quad\text{in }\mathcal{H}^{\frac{3}{4}},\quad\text{uniformly in }j.

We can again let
k
go to infinity to conclude that

({\mathbf{W}}_{j},R_{j})-({\mathbf{W}},R)\rightarrow 0\quad\text{in }\mathcal{H}^{\frac{3}{4}},

which shows the continuous dependence on the data in
\mathcal{H}^{\frac{3}{4}}
.

(4)
Continuation of solutions.

Here we show that the solution can be continued for as long as
\max\{{\underline{A}^{\sharp}},{\underline{A}}\}
remains small and
{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}}(\gamma^{\frac{1}{4}}+{\underline{A}_{\frac{1}{4}}})\in L^{1}_{t}
.
This is a direct consequence of the energy estimates Theorem
1.3
.
∎
